| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/ncm_structural_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/ncm_structural_proposer.py) | Two-tier Next-Concept Modeling (NCM) structural proposer: decomposes scientific discovery into qualitative macro-concept archetypes (conserved oscillator, continuum transport, rational saturation, cyclic regimes) before micro-parameter estimation and sparsity pruning. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/sindy_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/sindy_proposer.py) | SINDy-style sparse identification proposer: builds polynomial/trig library over state variables, applies sequential thresholded least squares for parsimonious ODE/PDE discovery with optional Rust acceleration. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/reservoir_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/reservoir_proposer.py) | Echo State Network (reservoir computing) proposer: fixed random recurrent dynamics with ridge regression readout for chaotic/time-series systems where exact symbolic forms are hard to find. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/basis_regression_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/basis_regression_proposer.py) | Basis function regression proposer (RBF/polynomials): fast screening step for guiding symbolic search with ridge regression over basis expansions. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/epistemic/gp_uncertainty.py`](../apedp_dyn/epistemic/gp_uncertainty.py) | Gaussian Process uncertainty quantification: RBF kernel GP regressor with Cholesky, Bayesian optimization with expected improvement for distinguishing interpolative fits from extrapolative predictions. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/epistemic/bayesian_active_learning.py`](../apedp_dyn/epistemic/bayesian_active_learning.py) | Bayesian active learning loop: uses GP uncertainty and acquisition functions to prioritize experiments/candidates for efficient discovery. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/pcmci_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/pcmci_proposer.py) | PCMCI+ (Tigramite, optional dependency) as a proposer for lagged time series: keeps the target's direct lagged parents after conditioning on the others, fits a linear model on them, emits one `CandidateProposal` whose score uses the past only. Linear dependence only (partial correlation) and no hidden common cause assumed; a pure interaction is invisible to it. Tests: `tests/test_llm_and_pcmci_proposers.py` (drops a 0.6-correlated echo variable) | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/llm_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/llm_proposer.py) | A caller-supplied language model as an equation proposer (no network code here): asks for skeletons with free constants, fits them, feeds scores back. Guards: variables are shown as `x0…`, never by column name; a recitation probe before any data marks proposals the model already knew as `recited_before_data`; only whitelisted text is ever parsed. Tested with a scripted stand-in; no real model has been run through it. Tests: `tests/test_llm_and_pcmci_proposers.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/llm_clients.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/llm_clients.py) | A `prompt -> reply` callable for the language-model proposer over NVIDIA's hosted, OpenAI-compatible API (`nvidia_client`), with request spacing and retries for the rate-limited free tier, and `pick_model`, which returns the first preferred model that is listed and actually answers. The key is read from `NVIDIA_API_KEY` at call time and never written to a file, a log line or an error message. Used by `scripts/run_llm_proposer_benchmark.py` (`.github/workflows/llm-proposer.yml`), which puts the model in PySR's place in the main search path with variables anonymised and no problem description. **No run with a real model yet:** the API is not reachable from the authoring environment and the workflow needs the repository secret. Tests: `tests/test_llm_clients.py` (no network) | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/pysr_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/pysr_proposer.py) | **The main symbolic search since 2026-11-19.** PySR wrapped as a `ProposerBase`: fits on the development rows it is given and returns its hall of fame as `CandidateProposal` objects (PySR's own pick first), in the caller's feature names; it never sees a confirmation row and accepts nothing itself. Benchmark settings (serial, deterministic, wall-clock budget); optional `guesses` seed the search. PySR needs Julia and is an optional dependency: without it `PySRUnavailableError` is raised, no weaker engine is substituted. Chosen on PREREG-SRBENCH-EXTERNAL-2026-11 (73 and 68 of 119 Feynman laws against 24 for the best in-house engine). Tests: `tests/test_main_search.py` (stand-in regressor), `tests/test_pysr_integration.py` (the real PySR; run by `.github/workflows/main-search-smoke.yml`) | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/composed.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/composed.py) | Proposers that wrap or combine others, so that the frozen main search path gains abilities without being edited. `UnitsReducingProposer`: if the target has the units of a product `s` of the inputs and `p1, p2, ...` are their independent dimensionless products, every dimensionally consistent law is `y = s * F(p1, ...)`; the wrapped search looks for `F` on `y / s`, over fewer variables, and its formulas are returned in the original variables (113 of the 119 Feynman problems reduce, 26 of them to no variable at all). Wrong or missing units cost a search, not a wrong answer: the path's checks still decide. `PolynomialLibraryProposer`: sparse regression over monomials up to degree three with the threshold chosen by BIC, one candidate in milliseconds (the eleven polynomial components the path missed on ODEBench). `CompositeProposer`: several proposers' candidates round-robin without duplicates; a member that raises is recorded and does not cost the others theirs (audit M-17). Tests: `tests/test_composed_proposers.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/llm_controller.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/llm_controller.py) | A language model that configures a search instead of writing the equations (the design the published LLM systems use; PREREG-LLM-PROPOSER measured the other one). `ControlledSearchProposer` asks the model once per problem which unary operators to allow, up to four derived inputs to add and up to three guesses with free constants; every item is checked (operators against the search's own set, expressions by the whitelist parser of `llm_proposer`, finiteness on the data) before it is used, and an unreadable reply or an unreachable model leaves the default search and is recorded. The search runs on inputs plus derived inputs, seeded with the guesses; formulas come back in the original variables. Not measured yet with a real model. Tests: `tests/test_composed_proposers.py` **Result (PREREG-LLM-CONTROLLER-2026-11, run once, all three expectations held):** 173 of 266 recovered against 174 without the model, 1 of 207 reported laws not accurate, every reply readable; the one inaccurate law came from a configuration without the operator the law needs (audit M-20) (`preregistration/RESULT-LLM-CONTROLLER-2026-11.md`). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/portfolio.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/portfolio.py) | Several independent searches on separate cores, merged into one proposer. `PortfolioProposer` runs its members at the same time, each in its own process (`spawn`, so each child starts its own Julia runtime), and merges their proposals round-robin without duplicates up to `max_proposals`; a member that raises or does not answer in time is recorded in `failures` and is fatal only if every member fails. `pysr_members` is the simplest portfolio: the same PySR search with different seeds. It decides nothing: the merged candidates go to the same contrast confirmation. Why: the default route's search is one serial PySR search on one of the runner's four cores, and two differently configured runs of the same path recovered 174 and 173 of 266 SRBench runs and 191 between them. **Development (2026-11-20, 30 development problems, 60 runs):** four seeds recovered exactly what one search recovers (27 and 25; none gained, none lost; no member failed), so on tables seeds alone were not taken to a preregistered run and the members have to differ in kind (`deep_pass.mixed_members`). On ODEBench's 15 development systems the same four seeds did gain (28 and 23 of 31 against 25 and 22), which PREREG-ODE-REFINED-2026-11 tests on held-out systems. Benchmark: `scripts/run_srbench_portfolio.py`, workflow `.github/workflows/srbench-portfolio.yml`; [`PREREG-PORTFOLIO-SEARCH-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-PORTFOLIO-SEARCH-2026-11.md) tested the four different searches on the 103 held-out problems. **Result (run once, 2026-11-20, all four expectations held):** 73 and 63 recovered by form against 61 and 61; 118 of the single search's 122 recoveries kept; 1 of 224 reported laws not accurate; no narrow pass ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-PORTFOLIO-SEARCH-2026-11.md)). It is the default search stage where four cores are free (`discovery_l2/search_stage.py`). Tests: `tests/test_portfolio_proposer.py` (8). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/precision_pruning.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/precision_pruning.py) | `prune_to_precision`: the refined path's term pruning with the mean square floored at the rows' own variance (for window rows, `WindowRows.rate_sd`), and removal limited to terms no larger than about that precision. Written because the original rule kept terms that fit the error of the rate estimates on trajectory rows (audit M-15). Applied after the fact to the 195 laws PREREG-ODE-REFINED reported: 4 changed, recovered 165 to 167, wrong 10 to 9, none lost. That is a development result on systems whose outcomes were already known; the rule is not wired into a route and not measured blind. Tests: `tests/test_precision_pruning.py` (5). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/fit_guard.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/fit_guard.py) | `FitGuardProposer` wraps a search stage and drops, with a record, any proposal the path's own least-squares fit raises on or returns non-finite for, under both noise models, before the path sees it (audit M-24: three preregistered runs ended on such a candidate). The path polishes constants before its own fit, so the guard removes candidates that fail as proposed and does not prove the path cannot fail. Used by `discovery_l2/search_stage.py`. Not yet measured in a run of its own. Tests: `tests/test_fit_guard_proposer.py` (5). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/sparse_library.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/sparse_library.py) | `SparseLibraryProposer`: sequentially thresholded least squares over all monomials up to a degree, the threshold chosen by BIC, as a proposer. It is the reference engine of the ODE runs (`run_sparse_poly3`) and returns the same law first, then the next distinct supports on the threshold path. It can only propose polynomials and decides nothing. Used by `ode/second_engine.py`. Tests: `tests/test_ode_additions.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/structure_memory.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/structure_memory.py) | Memory of confirmed laws as a source of candidates. From each law the referee reported, its *motifs* are kept: the parts that depend on one variable, their plain pieces (factors, what a quotient divides by), with the variable and every constant removed (`2.3*x0/(x0 + 1.7)` gives `v/(C + v)`), and the whole law's shape; no constant's value is kept. For a new problem `grow` builds laws one motif at a time over its variables (add a scaled motif, multiply by one, multiply or divide by a constant plus one), fits every constant after each step and grows the best few further; a periodic motif's constant is started at a scan of frequencies. `StructureMemoryProposer` returns the best grown laws and every remembered whole shape that fits, as proposals for the path's confirmation; it decides nothing. It can only build what its motifs reach. Not measured in a preregistered run; under test in PREREG-MEMORY-2026-11. Tests: `tests/test_structure_memory.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/agentic_variation.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/agentic_variation.py) | A language-model agent as the variation operator of a search, after Agentic Variation Operators (Chen et al., 2026, arXiv:2603.24517). `Workbench` holds the development rows and every expression tried on them, whoever tried it; fitting one returns the error, which simple functions of the inputs the residual (data minus fit) still follows, and where the largest error sits. `run_session` lets the model act for a bounded number of steps (`FIT` an expression, read a note with `KNOW`, `SUBMIT`); only lines of those kinds are read, and an expression is used only if the whitelist parser of `llm_proposer` accepts it. `AgenticVariationProposer` alternates a search with agent sessions on one lineage. An expression has to earn its cost: from the cheapest upwards one is kept only if its error is at least 5 % lower than the last one kept; those are returned first, then the rest by fit, and the model is told the same rule (the first run with a real model wrote the true law in its first step and then decorated it with sines below the noise). The agent sees development rows only and decides nothing about acceptance; every structure fitted is counted in `selection_pool_size`; a law the model states before seeing data is marked `recited_before_data`; an unreachable or useless model costs the search nothing. **Not measured in a preregistered run.** A smoke run with the real model (`nemotron-3-ultra`, four invented laws, 2026-11-21) showed that it drives the loop: every session fitted 7 to 18 expressions, no reply was unusable, a call took 3 to 73 seconds. With a search beside it the true structure came back for three of the four laws, twice with a small spurious term (which led to the earn-its-cost rule). **Development run, the same day** (twenty development laws, noise 0 and 1 %, `benchmarks/agentic_variation_dev_study.json`): with the search 16 of 40 recovered against the default stage's 13 (four gained, one lost, run for run: not distinguishable from chance), the agent alone 1 of 40, no wrong law in any arm, 504 s a run against 277 s. The default stage was not given the extra time, so the run does not show that the agent adds anything a longer search would not. `last_run["returned"]` now records, for every structure handed on, whether the agent, the search or a seed first wrote it. Tests with a scripted model: `tests/test_agentic_variation.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/agentic_stage.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/agentic_stage.py) | `agentic_search_stage`: the default stage's searches (four side by side where four cores are free, else one) with `AgenticVariationProposer` around them and the fit guard outermost. Not the default and not measured: nothing selects it unless a caller asks, and it costs more wall time than the default stage for the same search budget. Tests: `tests/test_agentic_variation.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/evolution/restricted.py`](../apedp_dyn/evolution/restricted.py) | The subset of Python that code written by a model may use, checked from its syntax tree before anything runs (`check`). Allowed: function definitions (nested ones and lambda), assignments, loops, comprehensions, `try/except` with a named exception, `raise`, `assert`, arithmetic, calls, f-strings, `with np.errstate`. Refused: any import, class, decorator, `global`, `del`, `yield`, `finally` (a `return` inside one would swallow the jail's time limit), any name or attribute that starts with an underscore, any attribute outside `ALLOWED_ATTRIBUTES` (array methods and the computing parts of `numpy` and `math`; no `np.load`, `.tofile`, `.format`), any assignment to an attribute, any name not defined in the code and not one of `ALLOWED_BUILTINS`, `np` or `math`. Type annotations are not checked because the jail compiles with deferred annotations (`COMPILE_FLAGS`) and never evaluates them. `describe()` gives the rules in words for a model. The first of two barriers; adding an attribute to the list is a security decision. Design and limits: [SEARCH_EVOLUTION.md](SEARCH_EVOLUTION.md). Tests: `tests/test_evolution.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/evolution/jail.py`](../apedp_dyn/evolution/jail.py) | Runs a checked candidate in a process of its own (`run`; `run_unchecked` is the same without the check, for testing this barrier alone). The candidate is never imported into the caller. It goes, with the arguments of every call, to `_jail_child.py` in a fresh interpreter with an environment of the jail's own making (`environment()`: thread counts, a fixed hash seed, the loader's library path where set; no variable of the caller), a temporary working directory, and a wall-clock limit on the batch. Each call has its own time limit; a call that keeps computing inside one operation ends its process, and the calls that remain run in a new one (`MAX_RESTARTS`), so one bad problem costs one answer. Returns a `JailRun` of `CallResult`s: JSON values, which the caller has yet to interpret. An answer of more than 200,000 characters is not read unless the caller's `result_chars` allows more (a task whose answers are whole circuits does). **Limit:** a Linux process with resource limits, not a container. Tests: `tests/test_evolution.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/evolution/_jail_child.py`](../apedp_dyn/evolution/_jail_child.py) | The jailed process. Imports nothing of this repository. Reads one request, loads everything a candidate can reach, then locks itself down: file size limit zero (nothing can be written), no new file descriptor (no file, pipe or socket can be opened), no new process (and, if started by root, it first stops being root, because root is exempt from that limit), an address-space cap and a CPU cap. For every call it defines the candidate afresh in a namespace holding `np`, `math` and the allowed builtins (`print` does nothing), seeds numpy's random numbers the same way, and reports one JSON line. The time limit is raised as a `BaseException`, so `except Exception` cannot swallow it; a profiling timer with its default action ends the process when a call is stuck where no exception can reach. numpy's compiled code fetches some of its own helpers by name on first use, through the builtins of whoever is running; the namespace therefore carries an import helper that hands out parts of numpy already loaded and nothing else, and that a candidate cannot name. Tests: `tests/test_evolution.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/evolution/operator.py`](../apedp_dyn/evolution/operator.py) | The loop in which a model rewrites a piece of the search and a fixed judge scores each version, after Agentic Variation Operators (Chen et al., 2026). A `Task` fixes the `Contract` (which function, what it must do, and what the model is told it is improving: `subject`) and the judge (`evaluate`: code in, `Evaluation` out). `Archive` keeps every version scored, with code, parent, score and what it failed on. `vary` shows the model the contract, the rules of the restricted language, the leading versions, the latest refusals, one parent's code and the judge's words on it; the reply's last fenced block is the new version; a refusal or a failure everywhere goes back for repair, a repeat of a scored version is not scored again. `evolve` runs variations from the leading versions in turn until a step budget or a deadline. The model chooses no winner and installs nothing; a reply is only ever parsed in the caller's process. **No real model has run this loop.** Tests with a scripted stand-in: `tests/test_evolution.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/evolution/evolved_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/evolution/evolved_proposer.py) | The first piece of the search the loop may rewrite: `propose(X, y, names)`, which returns up to four candidate laws as text. `read_candidates` is the only door from a candidate to the rest of the system: each string passes `safe_to_parse` (a power needs a variable in its base and a small or variable exponent, since the whitelist parser evaluates whole-number arithmetic exactly and `9**9**9` would stall it; only the syntax is read) and then the whitelist parser of `llm_proposer`; whatever is refused is dropped and counted. `CandidateGeneratorTask` is the judge: on problems with known laws it counts where the law is among the candidates by a criterion the caller supplies, then where a candidate is accurate, then fewer candidates; it names the laws a version missed. `EvolvedProposer` runs one checked version in the jail on the rows it is given and returns `CandidateProposal`s that decide nothing. The judge's score says whether a law is proposed, not which law the path reports. Tests: `tests/test_evolution.py` **Since 2026-11-23 the laws that score a version are never shown to the model**: the feedback named the true law of every scored problem a version missed, and a scripted adversary that kept a library of those laws raised its score without proposing any new law (audit M-37). `teaching` problems are run and their laws shown, and they count for nothing. `ACCURATE_R2` is `discovery_checks.DISCOVERY_ACCURACY_R2`. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/evolution/seed_generator.py`](../apedp_dyn/evolution/seed_generator.py) | `SEED_GENERATOR`: the version of `propose` every run of the loop starts from, as text. One fixed shape of each variable, added up with fitted constants; no constant inside a function, no product, no ratio. It proposes none of the 80 training and validation laws of `scripts/run_search_evolution.py`, so what a model adds is the model's. Text and not a module on purpose: a candidate is never imported. Tests: `tests/test_evolution.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/evolution/circuit_text.py`](../apedp_dyn/evolution/circuit_text.py) | The text form of the competition's circuits (`.kmx`: one instruction a line), read strictly. A circuit's text and its binary file are the same list of instructions (one converted into the other and back gives the same bytes), so a model that is to improve a circuit can be handed lines and asked for lines; lines written by a model are untrusted data on their way to a parser written in C++, and this module stands between the two. `read` accepts a line only if it is one of the gate, measurement, bit, condition and note instructions, written exactly as the simulator writes it (`parse_line`), and `written` writes it out again; anything else is refused with the line number and the reason: the lines that declare the input register (`header` writes those), `DEBUG_PRINT`, `MR` (which only a patched simulator has), a qubit named twice, an index beyond the cap, an angle in any form but plain decimals, a condition never closed, a line that is not text. `profile` counts what can be counted without running anything: the width the cost multiplies by (highest qubit named, plus one), `held` (the input plus the most working qubits in use at once, a qubit being free again after its `HMR`; where it is below the width, renaming alone makes the circuit cheaper), the costly gates and how many of them a shot may skip (`under_a_condition`), the rotations by kind (`rotation_kind`), and the same along the circuit and by the stages its notes mark. It loads no simulator and does not judge whether a circuit computes anything. Tests: `tests/test_circuit_evolution.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/evolution/circuit_task.py`](../apedp_dyn/evolution/circuit_task.py) | The competition's verifier as the judge of the variation loop (`operator.py`). A candidate is code in the restricted subset, run in the jail, defining `rewrite(lines, facts)`: it is handed the lines of a circuit that passes (`Start`) and returns the lines of a circuit; the seed returns them unchanged. `RewriteTask.evaluate` reads what comes back with `circuit_text.read` (a refusal goes back to the model with its reason and never reaches the checker), adds the register lines itself, writes the file and hands it to a `Checker` on one `Scope` after the other, cheapest first (`QUICK` 4,096 inputs, `WIDE` every input up to 2^20, `WHOLE` every input of the benchmark at 64 shots, run only for a circuit that would lead). Score: `(scopes passed, -cost)`, the cost being the checker's; a circuit that fails never outranks one that passes. **The cost is a sample** (how many costly gates a shot executes depends on its measurement outcomes, seeded by the circuit's bytes), so a circuit leads only if it is cheaper than the leading one by `MIN_GAIN` (0.02 %) and one inside that band counts as equal to it; without the band a loop climbs on the draw. The same circuit is checked once, whatever code returned it. `official_checker` is Benchmark I's verifier through `quantum_compilation.referee` against the pinned contract, **never with a salt** (`Scope` has no such field): a salted run on every input is a confirmation the ledger allows once, and a loop must not be able to ask for it; `receipt_checker` turns the referee's receipts into verdicts and records every one in a `QuantumCandidateLedger`. `circuit_file_as_text` has the pinned simulator write a circuit file as text, in a process of its own. **No model has driven it, and the official path has not run here**: the repository does not hold the simulator. Tests with a small simulator of the same instructions as the stand-in checker, and one opt-in test with the real verifier: `tests/test_circuit_evolution.py` **Since 2026-11-23 a circuit is drawn `REDRAWS` (4) times before it may lead or tie**: the same circuit with a note of the judge's own, each a fresh draw of the checker's cost. Its score is the mean. If its own draws differ by more than `MIN_GAIN`, it counts as equal to the leading circuit. A scripted adversary returning the given circuit with new notes led on lucky draws before (audit M-38). This assumes the verifier seeds on a note's bytes; `draws_all_equal` in the record says when it does not. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/redteam/counterfeit.py`](../apedp_dyn/redteam/counterfeit.py) | Counterfeit laws: a different form that fits the same rows almost as well as the true law. A law is a *recipe* (the invented-law grammar's building blocks, joined left to right); a counterfeit is the recipe with one block exchanged for another kind, or one summed block left out, and every constant then fitted to the true law itself over the domain. `distance` is what separates the two at best, as a share of the law's spread. `counterfeits` returns every exchange between 0.1 % and 30 %. It does not make counterfeits that differ from the law only outside the domain, or that lean on an input the law does not use. Tests: `tests/test_red_team.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/redteam/worlds.py`](../apedp_dyn/redteam/worlds.py) | A `World` for a referee under test: a law, the candidates a search is said to have returned (counterfeits, with or without the law), how many rows, how they are sampled (`uniform`, `narrow`, `clustered`, `correlated`) and what noise they carry (`gaussian`, `student`, `proportional`, `outliers`). Everything random comes from the world's own seed, so a world that defeats a referee can be written in a line and run again. Test rows are drawn the way the other rows are, without noise: a law is only called wrong for what it gets wrong where the data were. Tests: `tests/test_red_team.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/redteam/attack.py`](../apedp_dyn/redteam/attack.py) | The search over worlds. An `Arena` fixes which settings may vary: `measured` is the conditions of the preregistered runs (a wrong law there contradicts what they measured); `few_rows`, `sampling`, `noise_shape` and `noise_level` each leave those conditions in one direction. `draw` makes a world at random in an arena, `change` alters one thing and stays inside it, `climb` keeps the worlds with the worst outcome and changes those again. How bad an outcome is comes from the caller, so the same search serves any decision. Tests: `tests/test_red_team.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/redteam/plain_selection.py`](../apedp_dyn/redteam/plain_selection.py) | What a search does without a referee, for comparison on the same worlds and candidates: `best_fit` (constants refitted, lowest BIC, always reported) and `validated` (the same choice, reported unless its error on rows it has not seen is larger than on the rows it was fitted to, F test at the canonical level). Neither can say "none of these". Tests: `tests/test_red_team.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/evolved_stage.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/evolved_stage.py) | `evolved_search_stage`: the default stage's searches with one checked, jailed version of `propose` beside them, their candidates handed on in turn (`SideBySide`), the fit guard screening the generator's as it screens the searches'. Not the default and not measured; no version has been adopted. The path confirms at most `MAX_PROPOSALS` candidates of a stage, so each one the generator supplies takes the place of one of the searches' lower-ranked candidates: a comparison against the default stage has to measure that in both directions. Tests: `tests/test_evolution.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/outcome_forecast.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/outcome_forecast.py) | The system's forecast of its own results. `law_features` turns a law into numbers (size, functions, polynomial degree, inputs, noise); `OutcomeForecaster` learns from runs on record, per route, the chance that a law is recovered, that a law is reported, and that a wrong one is reported (one logistic regression per outcome, a route indicator when routes are pooled, a smoothed frequency for an outcome that never or always occurred); `Forecast` gives the chances per problem, the totals and an interval around each total, widened by how far whole runs have differed from their forecasts; `baseline` is the forecast that only knows the averages; `score` compares a forecast with what happened (Brier score, total against interval); `hardest` picks the problems least likely to be recovered, for practice. The features come from the true law, which a benchmark knows and the route does not: this forecasts runs on benchmarks, not on real data. **Backtest, not a test** (every outcome was known): on the table benchmark, where the same laws recur under other routes, Brier 0.12 to 0.16 against 0.21 to 0.25 for the averages; across kinds of dynamical systems worse than the averages in three of six cases; a whole run's recovery rate differed from its forecast by 10.6 percentage points (root mean square), so the unwidened interval held the total in 4 of 10 cases. The test is a forecast filed before a run. Tests: `tests/test_outcome_forecast.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/deciding_measurement.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/deciding_measurement.py) | Where rival laws disagree most. `disagreement` gives, per point, the largest difference between two rivals in units of the measurement's error; `deciding_points` picks the points of a box where they disagree most, kept apart from each other (for inputs that can be set: an experiment); `deciding_rows` picks rows of a pool (for inputs that can only be observed later: a market, a survey); `random_points` is the comparison; `describe` says what to measure in a sentence. It cannot know of a law that is not among the rivals, and it decides nothing: the measurement goes to the path's confirmation like any row. Used by `ode/chosen_experiment.py`. Tests: `tests/test_ode_additions.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/fast_first_pass.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/fast_first_pass.py) | The main search's first pass on a native kernel (`rust/apedp-kernel/src/first_pass.rs`), returning what the Python reference returns. `search_confirmed` has the signature of `confirmed_stop.search_confirmed` and its `GuidedResult`: the kernel fits the whole expression table in one pass, its numbers are used only to rule candidates out, each with a bound on how far the reference's value can be from it (`_doubt`: the conditioning of the fit, and how far two correct evaluations of the column itself can differ, which for an expression like `sin(exp(exp(x)))` is all of it; audit M-29), and every candidate a decision could still turn on is computed again with the reference's own functions, in the reference's order, so the result carries the reference's floats. `enabled()` makes `main_search` use it inside a `with` block by replacing one name at run time; no frozen file is edited, and without the kernel everything runs as before. `check=True` runs both and raises on any difference. The bound is an analysis with a safety factor, not a proof: the evidence is `scripts/check_fast_first_pass.py`. Tests: `tests/test_fast_first_pass.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouching.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouching.py) | What a route can vouch for: checks on a law the route is about to report and on the candidates it could not tell from it, built from the red-team campaign's findings (audit M-31, M-32). `structure_test`: the path's own residual statistic (leave-one-out R² of a residual from its nearest rows' residuals) at 8, 32 and 128 neighbours, each against its distribution under random flips of the residuals' signs, at the canonical test level; the path's check compares the 8-neighbour value with 0, where noise alone gives about -1/8. `vouch(law, others, names, X, y, X_more)`: members that leave structure are dropped; if those that remain differ from each other by more than `ACCURACY_R2` on every input at hand, nothing is reported and the class is named; if the route's law was dropped, the simplest member that remains is reported in its place; then `power_to_see` plants an error just large enough to make the law count as wrong, in the shape of each rival's difference and of low-order polynomials of the inputs, and the law is withheld if the test would have missed it more often than the canonical power minimum allows, or if its constants cannot be fitted that accurately from these rows. Every refusal carries its reason; nothing reads the truth or rows the path was not given. Every choice was made on the campaign's worlds, whose answers were known (replay in `benchmarks/vouching_dev_replay.json`). **Run once on 60 laws drawn afterwards** (PREREG-COUNTERFEIT-2026-11, all five expectations held): of the route's 16 wrong laws, all at 5 % noise, it withheld 14; it kept 139 of 146 right laws and turned 7 other reports into the law; 2 of the 55 laws it passed at 5 % noise were wrong. **In force for tables since 2026-11-22 through `discovery_l2/vouched_route.py`**; this module is frozen by that preregistration's digest. It finds no law, refuses a right one at its level (one in a hundred; none of 146 in that run), assumes errors symmetric about zero and independent from row to row, and its power rule is too confident for an error of another shape than those it plants (audit M-33). Result: [RESULT-COUNTERFEIT](../preregistration/RESULT-COUNTERFEIT-2026-11.md). Tests: `tests/test_vouching.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouching_v2.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouching_v2.py) | **The last checks, second version**, aimed at the faults the first showed on recorded runs (audit M-33, M-35). Two tests at half the canonical level each: the first version's neighbour test (`vouching.structure_test`), and `shape_test`, an F test of the residuals on polynomials of the inputs up to the fourth degree, each made orthogonal to the law's gradient in its constants (`gradient`, `basis`, `kept_shapes`: a shape the constants can follow is dropped). The power rule (`power_for`) takes that test's power from the noncentral F distribution for an error of the size that makes a law wrong; a law whose constants can follow every shape tried is withheld. The rivals are judged by the first version's class rule only: a test in their direction took the searches' noise for an error (refusing 8 of 90 right laws, and 7 after refitting on half the rows). A largest-residual test was tried and dropped. Does not repair M-34 (a pole a little off). Development replay (`scripts/replay_vouching_v2.py`): physics benchmark 192 of 193 recovered laws kept (first version 150); counterfeit benchmark 0 wrong of 193 reports (first version 2 of 191). **The last step of the route in force for tables since 2026-11-23** (`vouched_route_v2.py`), on 100 laws drawn after it (PREREG-VOUCHING-V2-2026-11, all five expectations held). Of handed counterfeits it passed no wrong law in 189 reports, where the route reported 19 in 231. Behind the search stage it passed no inaccurate law in 61 reports, where the route reported 11 of 31 at 5 % noise. It kept 26 of 27 recovered laws at noise up to 1 % and 3 of 4 at 5 %. Tested up to 5 % noise only; above it, weaker than the first version (M-36). Its docstring still says "three tests" in one place; the file is frozen. Result: [RESULT-VOUCHING-V2](../preregistration/RESULT-VOUCHING-V2-2026-11.md). Tests: `tests/test_vouching_v2.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/learning_loop.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/learning_loop.py) | **The system learning from the laws its referee confirms: development only, not in force.** `LearningState` holds two learners fed by the same laws. The memory of confirmed laws (`proposers.structure_memory.Memory`) grows with each learned law, where in force it is a file built once. The law language model (MMLM-L, `apedp_mmlm.law_language`) orders the first pass, which changes its speed, not its reach. A journal records every learned law with its source, `retract` unlearns one by rebuilding both from the seed and what is left, and `save` and `replay` keep the state. `discover_law` runs the refined path with the state's memory beside the given searches (in production the default stage without its frozen memory), and the state's order for the first pass. It then applies the last checks in force (`vouched_route_v2.checked`) and the measured range, and learns the law only if `learnable` allows: reported, vouched for by the last checks, and on a table inside or at the edge of the measured range. The referee, the checks and every threshold never learn. `discover_component` does the same on the ODE route, where only the memory learns. `LearningState(learners=...)` and `learnable(beyond_range=True)` take the loop apart for development streams. Development streams: with the default four searches on 30 new invented laws learning added nothing (empty memory 5 against 5 recovered; memory in force 8 against 10), and without searches from an empty memory 4 against 1. Tests: `tests/test_learning_loop.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/__init__.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/__init__.py) | **Recursive self-improvement on CPUs, inside a judge that never changes. Not in force; development only.** Three levels: finding laws (the route in force), improving the finder, improving how the finder is improved. Names the test of the word "recursive": `g_k`, fresh laws solved per CPU-hour at generation `k`, has to rise at every generation | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/levels.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/levels.py) | The levels. **Level 0** `solve`: one problem through `learning_loop.discover_law` with the searcher's memory, its library's candidates and (if it has one) a generator's code. **Level 1** `generation`, `run_level1`: study a curriculum batch and learn from what was solved; try copies of the searcher with moved settings on a batch drawn to decide, adopting a copy only if it solves more per CPU-hour with no more wrong laws; vary the generator's code if the owner gave a coding model (otherwise the record says it did not run); measure `g_k` on problems drawn for that alone. **Level 2** `run_level2`: `Meta` (variants, step, study and selection sizes, retrying failures) is varied, each way scored by the rise of `g` per CPU-hour it spent, the incumbent re-run on new problems every round. The judge's seal is checked at every generation | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/judge.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/judge.py) | **The fixed judge.** `verdict`: solved is a law the route reported that reaches `DISCOVERY_ACCURACY_R2` on clean rows it never saw; a reported law below it is wrong. `seal` / `check`: the SHA-256 of the referee package, both threshold specifications, the route, the last checks, the measured range, the learning gate, the curriculum, the progress test and the benchmark's fingerprints; a change raises `JudgeChanged`. `check_settings`: a searcher has five settings with bounds and nothing else, so a search over settings cannot reach a threshold | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/progress.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/progress.py) | The test of "recursive". `Measurement.g` is solved per CPU-hour with a binomial standard error. `verdict`: *recursive* only if `g` rose by more than two standard errors at every step over at least three generations and the share of wrong laws did not rise; *improved, not shown recursive* if only the last is above the first; else *not shown* | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/library.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/library.py) | **Library learning by compression** (DreamCoder's step, in a small form). `Library.learn` turns the parts of solved laws into blocks with holes (up to two inputs) and keeps those whose naming saves bits. `LibraryProposer` places blocks on the problem's inputs, joins the best two and three at a time, fits constants, ranks by description length, and drops a candidate that rewrites show equal to a better one or that a solver proves has a pole among the rows' range. It proposes; the referee decides | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/curriculum.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/curriculum.py) | **A self-made curriculum.** Invented laws at a level from 0 to 6 (inputs and blocks), drawn around the level the system solves about half the time; `measurement` draws from fixed levels so rates are comparable across generations. A level with no law left (level 0 has under ten) hands a study slot to the next level up and is recorded as used up; a measurement stops with an error instead. Study, selection and measurement never share a law. The benchmark's structures are left out by fingerprint; the benchmark file itself is never opened, so the forty held-back laws stay unread | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/egraph.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/egraph.py) | **Equality saturation.** An e-graph over the law grammar with rewrite rules (commutativity, associativity, distribution, powers, `exp` of a sum, constant folding; `log` of a product only with `positive=True`). `equal` answers `True` (the rewrites are a proof) or `None`, never `False`; `simplest` extracts the smallest form reached. Bounded by a node limit | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/smt.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/smt.py) | **Proofs with z3 on a box of inputs.** `pole_in_box`: a denominator that reaches zero, or a logarithm's argument that reaches zero or below, with a witness point; or a proof there is none. `equal_in_box`: two rational laws proved equal or told apart with a point. Polynomial parts with rational coefficients only; anything else, a time-out or a missing z3 is `None` (not decided). Not called by the referee or the last checks | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/recursion/mdl.py`](../apedp_dyn/recursion/mdl.py) | Description length in bits: a law's tree (one choice per node among the operations, inputs and library blocks; 16 bits a fitted constant) plus the Gaussian code length of its residuals. Chooses the library and orders its candidates; decides nothing the referee decides | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouching_v3.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouching_v3.py) | **The last checks, third version: development only, not in force.** The second version's outcome (`vouching_v2.vouch`, unchanged), then a pole rule (`after_second`). The chosen law is withheld if a denominator in it (the base of a negative power), or the argument of a logarithm, takes both signs, or zero, on the rows it was fitted on and the pool's inputs (`singular_parts`, `pole_among`). Such a law's values are unbounded among the rows, so a slightly misplaced pole makes it as wrong as one likes on rows near either pole (audit M-34, and most of M-36). It only withholds. It cannot see a denominator that touches zero without changing sign, nor poles of `tan` or `gamma`. Development replay (`scripts/replay_vouching_v3.py`; the rule was designed after reading the second red-team campaign, so that campaign's numbers are not a test of it). **Second red-team campaign:** it let through 15 wrong laws instead of 98 at noise up to 5 %, and 4 instead of 70 above it. It kept 714 instead of 738, and 12 instead of 20, right laws. **Physics benchmark:** it kept 187 of 193 recovered laws (the second version 192), withholding five laws that have a pole among the rows, among them `cot` and `sec`. **Counterfeit benchmark and first campaign:** the same as the second version. A switch to the first version above 5 % noise did worse on every set. Tests: `tests/test_vouching_v3.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/measured_range.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/measured_range.py) | Marks a table **inside**, at the **edge** of, or **outside** the conditions the route in force was measured on (PREREG-VOUCHING-V2: noise up to 5 % of the target's size, up to three inputs, 700 rows to fit on). It refuses nothing. The noise is estimated from the reported law's residuals on the rows it was fitted on, relative to the clean target's root mean square, with its standard error; within two standard errors of 5 % is the edge. A wrong law reads as noisier, so the mark errs towards outside. Gaussian noise and evenly spread rows cannot be checked from one table, and the mark says so. The measured conditions are constants that a test checks against the blind test's laws file. Development check (`scripts/check_measured_range.py`, `benchmarks/measured_range_dev.json`): of 807 recorded reports of laws that were not wrong, under the measured conditions, 5 were marked outside, all at 5 % noise. Of 311 recorded tables outside them, none was marked inside. Tests: `tests/test_measured_range.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouched_route_v2.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouched_route_v2.py) | **The route in force for tables since 2026-11-23**, as PREREG-VOUCHING-V2-2026-11 fixed before its run, in a new module around the frozen ones. `discover_law` is `default_route.discover_law` with the same arguments and record. On the refined path a reported law then goes through `vouching_v2.vouch` with the candidates the path could not tell from it, the rows it was fitted on (`vouched_route.rows_given` of them) and the pool's inputs. `checked` does that to a record, as `vouched_route.checked` does: the law stays, gives way to another member of its class, or is withheld as tentative with the reason. Checks that crash vouch for nothing. `version()`: `APEDP_TABLE_VOUCHING=off` gives no last checks; `=first` (or `=on`, which asked for the first version before) gives `vouched_route.checked`; anything else gives the second version. **Cost, stated first:** behind the search stage on 40 new laws it kept 26 of 27 recovered laws at noise up to 1 % and 3 of 4 at 5 %; of handed right laws 109 of 110 and 29 of 30. **Benefit:** no wrong law in 189 handed reports (the route: 19 in 231); behind the search, none of its 17 reports at 5 % noise inaccurate (the route: 11 of 31). **Open:** above 5 % noise it is weaker than the first version (M-36); a law with a pole can pass (M-34); tables up to three inputs, Gaussian noise, uniform rows. At import it checks that the checks in force use the named thresholds of `conf/discovery_checks.py`. It was put in force without the owner's word (AGENTS.md 1.5, written afterwards); `APEDP_TABLE_VOUCHING=off` undoes it. | `experimental` |

The owner's decision on PREREG-VOUCHED-SRBENCH-2026-11, whose third expectation missed: on SRBench behind the four searches the checks kept 150 of the 193 laws the route recovered (47 of 90 at 1 % noise) and stopped no inaccurate law (audit M-35). | [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouched_route.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/vouched_route.py) | **Out of the route in force since 2026-11-23, by the owner's decision; applied with `APEDP_TABLE_VOUCHING=first` through `vouched_route_v2.py`.** Its cost (PREREG-VOUCHED-SRBENCH-2026-11, one expectation missed): on SRBench behind the four searches the checks kept 150 of the 193 laws the route recovered, 47 of 90 at 1 % noise, and stopped no inaccurate law (audit M-35). Checks that crash vouch for nothing. The earlier routes (`main_search`, `l2`) are handed on as they were measured. `APEDP_TABLE_VOUCHING=off` gives the route without the checks. `rows_given`: the checks compare every pair of rows, so they read at most 1,500 rows and fewer where a table has more than seven inputs. **Limits stated with it:** measured on candidates handed over, uniform rows and Gaussian noise up to 5 %; not behind a search engine or on the physics benchmark, where what the checks cost in recovered laws is unknown; at 5 % noise 2 of 55 passed laws were wrong (interval to 12.5 %). | [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/stage_in_force.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/stage_in_force.py) | **The search stage in force for tables since 2026-11-22**, as PREREG-MEMORY-2026-11 fixed before its run, in a new module around the frozen ones: `search_stage(budget)` is `memory_stage.memory_search_stage`, the default searches with the memory's three grown laws beside them, taking turns, so a remembered law takes the place of one of the searches' lower-ranked candidates and never of their best. `APEDP_TABLE_MEMORY=off` gives the searches alone. Evidence: 40 laws drawn after the memory was written, both arms handed the same searched candidates: 23 of 80 recovered against 17, six gained, none lost, no wrong law in either arm. **Limits stated with it:** nearly all of the gain is without noise; the laws are of one grammar, which the memory's way of joining parts was written knowing; up to 60 s more per discovery. | [`apedp_dyn/epistemic/prediction_ledger.py`](../apedp_dyn/epistemic/prediction_ledger.py) | A hash-chained, append-only ledger of dated predictions: a claim fixes the law, what it predicts, the data source, the score, the bound and the due date; an outcome records the observed score and the data's hash. One outcome per claim, not before its due date; whether it held is computed from the claim's own bound; an unresolved claim past its date is counted as overdue; any edit or removal breaks the chain (`verify`). It decides nothing about a law and does not itself prove when an entry was written (a public commit does). No claims recorded yet. Tests: `tests/test_prediction_ledger.py` (7). | `experimental` |
| [`docs/COMPETITIVE_RESEARCH_2026-11.md`](COMPETITIVE_RESEARCH_2026-11.md) | Where the system stands against published equation-discovery methods, AI-scientist systems and automated replication work, from public sources read on 2026-11-20 (outside numbers not re-measured), and the order of work that follows. | `audit-only` |
| [`apedp_dyn/market/law_audit.py`](../apedp_dyn/market/law_audit.py) | An audit of published laws of asset returns on a daily panel. Six numbers, each compared with a published set: the Hill tail index over each asset's largest 5 % of absolute returns, the variance ratio of 5-day to 1-day returns, the decay exponent of the autocorrelation of absolute returns (from the variogram of their ranks, which needs no estimate of the mean), the correlation of a return with later absolute returns, the correlation of absolute return with relative volume, and as a control the lag-1 autocorrelation of absolute returns. Intervals come from resampling 90-day blocks of days, widened by a factor per number measured on simulated panels (`STRETCH`); the verdict is `fails` (interval wholly outside the published set), `survives` (wholly inside) or `not_rejected`. Two form tests score a power-law tail and a power-law decay against exponential rivals on a later period. Honest scope: the factors are as good as the simulated panels are like a market; the tail index over the largest 5 % is not the limiting exponent (2.3 where the shocks' exponent is 3); on five years of daily data the decay exponent's interval is twice the published range. `tests/test_market_law_audit.py` (50). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/market/law_audit_worlds.py`](../apedp_dyn/market/law_audit_worlds.py) | Simulated return panels in which each audited law holds or is broken by a known amount, on any panel's shape (assets that start late and stop early): Student shocks with one common factor, log-volatility with an exactly power-law, exponential or absent autocorrelation (circulant embedding), leverage through the surprise in the next day's common volatility (Durbin-Levinson), volume that follows volatility. Eighteen worlds. Used to measure how far the audit's intervals must be widened and how often its verdicts are right. Simpler than a market: no listing jumps, regime changes, weekly cycle or second factor. | `audit-only` |
| [`apedp_dyn/market/law_audit_more.py`](../apedp_dyn/market/law_audit_more.py) | Six further published regularities, audited as the first five were and built around the frozen `law_audit.py` (same cells, block resampling and verdict rule). Seven numbers, each claimed to be above zero: the share of an asset's largest moves that are losses minus one half (`asymmetry`); the share of large daily moves minus the share of large weekly moves (`gaussianity`); the share of large moves before minus after dividing each return by a volatility estimate from earlier days (`conditional`), and what is left after that division beyond what independent normal returns in the same cells leave (`still_heavy`); how well a week's net move predicts the next week's daily moves minus the reverse (`cascade`); the rank correlation across assets of the last one to four weeks' return with the next week's (`momentum`); the rank correlation of a day's taker-buy imbalance with the next day's return (`flow`). A large move exceeds four times the asset's median move; the medians are fixed on the whole panel and every share is corrected to first order for where a resample's own medians would lie, without which the interval of `conditional` is several times too wide. `load_flow_panel` reads the taker-buy column. Tests: `tests/test_market_law_audit_more.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/market/law_audit_more_worlds.py`](../apedp_dyn/market/law_audit_more_worlds.py) | The first batch's simulated worlds with six more dials, applied to the returns of a first-batch panel: `skew` (losses scaled up, gains down), `drift` (a wandering expected return: momentum), `transitory` (a fading price component: reversal), `cascade` (last week's net move raises volatility), `flow` (the surprise in order flow carries into the next day), `stable` (returns replaced by independent stable-law draws: Mandelbrot's hypothesis, the rival of aggregational Gaussianity). Every panel gets a taker-buy volume. With every dial at zero the panel is the first batch's, unchanged. Seven calibration worlds and sixteen power worlds; `holds` says by dial whether a claim is true in a world. Each mechanism acts on one asset at a time; real markets need not produce these numbers that way. | `audit-only` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/search_stage.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/search_stage.py) | The default route's search stage, as PREREG-PORTFOLIO-SEARCH-2026-11 fixed for the case its expectations held: `default_search_stage(budget)` returns, inside a `FitGuardProposer`, the four different searches of `deep_pass.mixed_members` in a `PortfolioProposer` where four cores are available (`available_cores`, the process's affinity), and one PySR search otherwise. The UI's discovery endpoint uses it. Not measured for ODE systems or series. Tests: `tests/test_search_stage.py` (3). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/deep_pass.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/deep_pass.py) | The exhaustive first pass one or two levels deeper, as a portfolio member. `DeepPassProposer` runs the confirmed-stop search with the size prior at 8 nodes for one variable and 7 for two or three (420 567, 220 491 and 502 733 candidates; nothing for more variables) and proposes the one law it stops on. Deterministic, one core, no Julia. **Development (2026-11-20):** on the 18 development runs the first passes alone miss it recovered 3, one of them (`feynman_test_5` without noise) recovered by no PySR run of this repository; PySR recovers 10 of the 18, and at 1 % noise the deeper pass returned four laws that fit and are not the law, so it is one voice in a portfolio and never the only search. `mixed_members`: default PySR, this pass, PySR with algebraic operators only, default PySR with the next seed. Tests: `tests/test_deep_pass_proposer.py` (6). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/llm_https.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/llm_https.py) | The language-model client for an https service only: `require_https`, `nvidia_client`, `available_models`. `llm_clients.py` hands its `base_url` to `urlopen` with the API key in a header and is frozen by two preregistrations; these wrappers refuse an `http://`, `file://` or schemeless address before any request (audit M-21). New code calls these. Tests: `tests/test_security_scan_baseline.py` (10). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/causal_graph.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/causal_graph.py) | The whole lagged causal graph of a multivariate series from one PCMCI+ run (`discover_graph`), plus a pass for links that act through an even function of the source, which partial correlation cannot see (`even_links`, `discover_graph_with_even_links`). Two baselines measure what the search is worth: a full vector autoregression with t-tests (`var_links`) and marginal lagged correlation (`correlation_links`). Assumes no hidden common cause, no links within a time step and a fixed graph. Preregistered test on simulated series with a known graph: [`PREREG-GRAPHS-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-GRAPHS-2026-11.md) (`scripts/run_prereg_graphs.py`, worlds in `scripts/causal_graph_worlds.py`). Tests: `tests/test_causal_graph.py` **Result (run once, all four expectations held):** on 60 series PCMCI+ has precision 0.963 and recall 0.937 (0.973 and 0.992 on linear and heavy-tailed series) against 0.901 and 0.944 for the vector autoregression and 0.229 precision for marginal correlation; with the even pass 57 of 58 even links are found (7 without it), at a cost in precision on heavy-tailed series (0.899 against 0.962) ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-GRAPHS-2026-11.md)). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/rime_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/rime_proposer.py) | RIME symbolic-mechanism proposer: emits closed-form equations from typed mechanism graphs, adjudicated by the same frozen referee. **Not for discovering laws from tabular data since 2026-11-19:** 24 of 532 SRBench runs recovered (4.5 %, PREREG-SRBENCH-BASELINE-2026-11) against 160 of 266 for PySR; use `apedp_dyn.discovery_l2.main_search`. Still used by the time-series repair path (`rime_staged_search`, `rime_repair_engine`) and the QED arms, which that benchmark did not cover. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/stem_proposers.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/stem_proposers.py) | Modular multi-engine STEM proposers (Option 2): Hamiltonian energy-conserving mechanical systems, solenoidal divergence-free fluid velocity fields, and compartmental epidemiological dynamics. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/proposers/invariant_transfer_proposer.py`](../apedp_dyn/proposers/invariant_transfer_proposer.py) | Active cross-regime invariant transfer proposer: IRM-penalized representations with worst-regime retention gating, Cross-Regime Generalization Index (CGI) ranking, and active extrapolation-barrier checks. | `production` |

| [`apedp_dyn/sandbox/canonical_ledger.py`](../apedp_dyn/sandbox/canonical_ledger.py) | Canonical execution ledger with additive log accounting, stock-split invariance, and fee models. | `production` |

It is still what the UI, the physics endpoint and the refusal-memory ladder call; moving those is open work (audit M-11). | `production` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/multiterm_engine.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/multiterm_engine.py) | Multi-term superposition discovery engine for L2 dynamics (structure-aware quota screen). | `production` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/refusal_memory.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/refusal_memory.py) | Discovery loop that learns from refusals without fitting to them: climbs a fixed ladder of hypothesis spaces (strict grammar; coefficients that carry units; two-term superposition) on a proposer-validation half of the data and confirms the chosen rung once on the other half, which is then spent (`ConfirmationAlreadySpent`). Every round is a trial in `CandidateTrialLedger` under one `search_id`. Memory is keyed by domain, reads only verdict fields (`READABLE_FIELDS`), and skips a rung only after two datasets of the same domain outgrew it. Tests: `tests/test_refusal_memory.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/work_queue.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/work_queue.py) | Spreads discovery jobs over processes or machines that share a filesystem: chunks are claimed by atomic rename, results written by atomic replace, dead workers' chunks requeued. Workers never write the trial ledger; `collect` records every result in job order, so the ledger does not depend on the number of workers. CPU only, no broker. CLI: `python -m apedp_dyn.discovery_l2.work_queue worker --root <dir>`. Tests: `tests/test_discovery_work_queue.py` (one worker and three processes give identical candidates, verdicts and trial counts) | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/contrast_confirmation.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/contrast_confirmation.py) | Decides between rival laws by measuring where they disagree instead of on a random holdout. Rows are chosen from inputs alone (design hashed before any target is read); the power of each comparison is computed first, and a pair that cannot be told apart at the canonical Diebold–Mariano error rate is reported as equivalent on the domain without spending a target; the survivor is then tested against its own development error, and a failure is reported as `no_survivor` (the true law is outside the candidates). Predictions are charged for coefficient uncertainty. Preregistered test [`PREREG-CONTRAST-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-CONTRAST-2026-11.md) (`scripts/run_prereg_contrast.py`): true law uniquely confirmed in 63 of 80 runs on fresh laws against 54 for a random-rows control and 28 referee promotions, no wrong law named; with the true law removed it still named a wrong law alone in 19 of 80, so `unique` is relative to the candidates given ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-CONTRAST-2026-11.md)). Development study: `scripts/study_contrast_confirmation_dev.py`. Tests: `tests/test_contrast_confirmation.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/exhaustive_search.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/exhaustive_search.py) | Exhaustive search over every expression up to a node limit (bottom-up, duplicates up to scale and offset removed by a numeric fingerprint: 61,717 candidates for 3 variables at 6 nodes in 1.2 s), each fitted as `a·f + b`, leaders also summed with every simple term, ranked by a two-part description length with a BIC parameter cost. In the spirit of Exhaustive Symbolic Regression but not that method: no free constants inside expressions, greedy over sums. The candidate list is deterministic, so `score_slice`/`merge`/`slice_job` split it over `work_queue.py` workers with the same answer as one process. Not yet benchmarked beyond the four disclosed smoke problems (2 of 4 recovered, including the Gaussian `feynman_I_6_2a`). Tests: `tests/test_exhaustive_search.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/contrast_resolution.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/contrast_resolution.py) | The step after a referee abstention where more can be measured: takes the engine's own candidates to contrast confirmation and returns the next action (`confirmed`, `equivalent_class`, `unchallenged`, `widen_search`) beside the referee's untouched verdict. `climb` runs the three rungs of `refusal_memory.LADDER` (and, given a domain and dataset id, skips rungs the refusal memory says the domain has outgrown and records each round for it), moving up only on `widen_search` (no candidate survived), never because of `confirmed`, as PREREG-CONTRAST-2026-11 requires; each rung is one trial in the ledger. Promotes nothing. Tests: `tests/test_contrast_resolution.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/guided_search.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/guided_search.py) | The exhaustive search in order of a prior (`apedp_mmlm.equation_language.search_prior`), stopping when the best candidate's residuals cannot be predicted from their nearest neighbours' residuals; the order is priced once per prior and reused. If nothing stops it, the full search's answer is returned. PREREG-MMLM-S2-2026-11, 128 runs: 121 laws recovered against 123 for the plain search in 3.5 % of its time (0.3 % of candidates fitted), with 7 early stops on a near-miss law (5.5 %, one over the preregistered limit). Tests: `tests/test_guided_search.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/confirmed_stop.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/confirmed_stop.py) | An early stop for the guided search that has to be confirmed: after the first candidate with no residual structure at position c, the search continues to max(3c, c + 300) and returns the structure-free candidate with the shortest description length. Written because the first-stop rule returned a near-miss law in 7 of 128 runs of PREREG-MMLM-S2. Tested in [`PREREG-MMLM-L-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-MMLM-L-2026-11.md). Tests: `tests/test_confirmed_stop.py` **Result:** with the law prior 96 of 100 laws recovered (plain search 93), 4 wrong early stops (limit 5), 4.3 % of the plain search's time ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-MMLM-L-2026-11.md)). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/main_search.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/main_search.py) | The main search path for discovering a law from tabular data: (1) the exhaustive search in the order of a prior with the confirmed early stop; (2) PySR (or any `ProposerBase`), seeded with the first pass's best expression, when the first pass did not stop early; (3) contrast confirmation over the candidates of both stages, reading pool targets once each; (4) an adequacy check: the survivor must leave no structure in its development residuals, otherwise the outcome is `widen_search` and the law is reported as rejected. Records one trial in the ledger in the form `apedp_mmlm.law_ledger` learns from. Does not use the template search or the referee's sole-survivor rule (audit M-10). Since 2026-11-19 it also: confirms fits that are exact to numerical precision directly on pool rows (noise-free data); refits the constants inside searched expressions (`polish`); reports the simplest member of an equivalent class; and requires the law to predict at least as well as a nearest-neighbour smoother (`adequacy`), added after a law with test R² 0.95 passed the residual check alone on `strogatz_bacres1`. **Being measured as a whole** by [`PREREG-MAIN-SEARCH-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-MAIN-SEARCH-2026-11.md) (`scripts/run_srbench_main_search.py`, `.github/workflows/srbench-main-search.yml`), which also measures the in-house GA/GP for the first time. Tests: `tests/test_main_search.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/series_route.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/series_route.py) | The route for a noisy series in time order (return-like data: a law explains a few percent of the variance at most, noise heavy-tailed and of changing scale). On such data the main search path fails both ways: its first pass alone reported a law in ten of twelve development worlds that contained none, and with PySR's candidates its confirmation step refused every candidate in seven of twenty that did contain one. So the path only supplies candidates. One is chosen inside the development period (refit on the first two thirds, scored on the last third; candidates that do not beat the mean are dropped; the simplest within one standard error of the best is taken), and that one gets a single Diebold-Mariano test against the development mean on the following period (`apedp_referee/novelty_adjudication.evaluate_predictive_incrementality`, level from the canonical specification). Outcome `predictive_law` or `no_predictive_law`; the path's own verdict is kept as the ungated reading. The module decides nothing by itself and a test consumes the following period. Benchmark: `scripts/run_market_planted_benchmark.py` on `scripts/market_planted_worlds.py`, `preregistration/PREREG-MARKET-PLANTED-2026-11.md`. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/ui/route_endpoint.py`](../apedp_dyn/ui/route_endpoint.py) | What the UI shows of the measured discovery routes. `route_reading` runs the route in force for tables (`discovery_l2/table_route.py`: the default route, the refined path; the last checks only on request since 2026-11-23) on tabular rows and returns a JSON-safe record: a law confirmed with the search stage run is `reported`; a law nothing challenged (audit M-14), found without PySR (M-19) or not vouched for by the last checks (M-31, M-32) is `tentative` with `tentative_reason`; `vouching` says what the checks did; a reading made without the PySR stage is marked `complete: false`. `series_reading` runs the series route on rows in time order (return-like data; audit M-16). `route_request` validates a request body; `status_block` is what `/api/status` says: default route, routes with the recorded result behind each, retired engines, and which endpoints still run the template engine alone. It decides nothing itself. The search stage runs only when a budget is given (`search_seconds`, or `APEDP_UI_SEARCH_SECONDS`), capped at 300 s; it is the stage in force (`discovery_l2/stage_in_force.py`: the searches with the memory of reported laws beside them). `status_block` carries `last_step`: whether the checks are in force (not, since the owner's decision of 2026-11-23), what they do, their cost first and the runs behind them. `mount` registers `POST /api/discover/route`; `ui/server.py` calls it, `status_fields` and `market_series_reading`, and `ui/physics_endpoint.py`. Tests: `tests/test_dyn_ui_route_endpoint.py` (16). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/refined_search.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/refined_search.py) | The main search path with what its benchmarks showed it lacks, added around it and not in it. `discover_refined` runs the frozen path with a composed search stage (the search inside the units wrapper, plus the polynomial library), always with the search stage on so that rivals exist, and writes the outcome down differently: the law is the confirmed form (one refitted coefficient per additive term) after backward elimination by BIC of every term the data do not notice (audit M-13 and M-15), and a law that nothing challenged is returned as `tentative_law`, not as `law` (M-14). Every verdict is still the frozen path's. Benchmark: `scripts/run_srbench_refined.py`, `preregistration/PREREG-REFINED-SEARCH-2026-11.md`. **Result (run once):** 212 laws reported in 266 SRBench runs, none inaccurate, 174 recovered (the main path: 186, 4, 152); held-out 61 and 61 against 55 and 52, one short of the 62 expected at noise 0 (`preregistration/RESULT-REFINED-SEARCH-2026-11.md`). Tests: `tests/test_composed_proposers.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/default_route.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/default_route.py) | The route in force for discovering a law from tabular data: the refined path since 2026-11-20 (the owner's decision on PREREG-REFINED-SEARCH's result, which missed one of three expectations by one law). `discover_law` runs `refined_search.discover_refined` unless `main_search` or `l2` is named or set in `APEDP_LAW_DISCOVERY_ROUTE`, and hands those two to the frozen `route.py` unchanged; the record has `route.discover_law`'s keys plus `tentative_law`, `tentative_reason`, `reported` and `complete`. With no proposer the refined route reports nothing: the measured path had PySR on every problem, and a law confirmed against the first pass and the polynomial library alone is returned as tentative with the reason (audit M-19). `units` and `target_units` reach the refined route. Since 2026-11-22 the route in force for a table is this one with a last step, `discovery_l2/vouched_route.py`, which the UI calls; this module is as it was measured. Tests: `tests/test_default_route.py` (8). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/guarded_l2.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/guarded_l2.py) | `GuardedL2DiscoveryEngine`: the template engine with the referee's structure guard (`epistemic/structure_guard.py`) on by default, the consequence PREREG-REFEREE-STRUCTURE-2026-11 fixed for its first two expectations holding. A subclass because `l2_engine.py` is frozen by that preregistration; `structure_rule=False` gives the frozen engine exactly. Used by `ui/physics_endpoint.py`. Callers on trajectory or time-ordered rows are not moved (the guard's permutation test assumes exchangeable rows); audit M-10 lists each. Tests: `tests/test_guarded_l2_engine.py` (4). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/route.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/route.py) | One entry point for discovering a law from tabular data and the switch between the two routes: `main_search`, or `l2` (the template ladder, `contrast_resolution.climb`). On the `l2` route, when the rungs are exhausted and a proposer is supplied, the widening step is the main search instead of a refusal. `DEFAULT_ROUTE` is `main_search` since 2026-11-19: PREREG-MAIN-SEARCH-2026-11 fixed expectations 1 to 3 as the condition and they held (4 of 186 reported laws not accurate, 152 the law; `preregistration/RESULT-MAIN-SEARCH-2026-11.md`); `APEDP_LAW_DISCOVERY_ROUTE=l2` chooses the ladder per deployment. The record carries `law` (the search's text) and `law_as_confirmed` (one refitted coefficient per additive term, the form confirmation was applied to; use it for prediction, audit M-13). The UI still calls the template engine directly. Tests: `tests/test_discovery_route.py` | `experimental` |

| [`apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/discovery_stability_harness.py`](../apedp_dyn/discovery_l2/discovery_stability_harness.py) | Multi-seed discovery stability harness and identifiability-matrix generator. | `audit-only` |
| [`rust/apedp-kernel/src/genetic.rs`](../rust/apedp-kernel/src/genetic.rs) | Rust-accelerated genetic algorithm evaluator with verified minimum-MSE selection, top-level trig frequency/phase template seeding (`sin\|cos(f*x + p)`, rate 0.18, `allow_trans`-gated; closes the frozen N04 unreachability forensics), and post-evolution constant polish (golden-section coordinate descent with shrinking windows + Powell conjugate-direction escape, strict-improvement replacements, affine readout refit at every probe; never-worsens A/B pinned). | `production` |
| [`rust/apedp-vqc/src/lib.rs`](../rust/apedp-vqc/src/lib.rs) | The `apedp_vqc` Python extension module (PyO3, six entry points): `run_report`, `equivalence_images`, `distribution`, `generators`, `expectation`, `gate_tables`. Structured answers are single JSON strings, so the FFI contract is a schema rather than a tuple order; a refusal is `NotImplementedError` and means "this path could not answer", never "this candidate is bad" | `experimental` |
| [`rust/apedp-vqc/src/stabilizer.rs`](../rust/apedp-vqc/src/stabilizer.rs) | The stabilizer tableau in Rust: `n` signed Pauli generators, GF(2) elimination over packed word vectors, the "first anticommuting generator" measurement with phase arithmetic mod 4, reset, exact `probability_of_bitstring`, and `2**n` enumeration for `n <= 12`. A port of `apedp_dyn/vqc/stabilizer.py` that keeps its ordering, so deterministic answers match exactly | `experimental` |
| [`rust/apedp-vqc/src/pauli.rs`](../rust/apedp-vqc/src/pauli.rs) | Signed Pauli strings and the Clifford conjugation tables, **derived** at first use from the 2x2 and 4x4 gate unitaries (as the Python tier derives them) instead of transcribed, with the quaternion product table built the same way | `experimental` |
| [`rust/apedp-kernel/src/video.rs`](../rust/apedp-kernel/src/video.rs) | Native video kernel: global PSNR and windowed grayscale SSIM, zero-copy Sliced Wasserstein-2, Rayon-parallel 2D bilinear sub-pixel coordinate warping (`warp_bilinear_2d`), central-difference optical flow divergence (`compute_optical_flow_divergence`), and disocclusion mask detector (`detect_disocclusion_mask`). Exact-parity pinned against Python reference. | `production` |
| [`apedp_dyn/video/decoupled_transport.py`](../apedp_dyn/video/decoupled_transport.py) | Decoupled Continuous Spatio-Temporal Transport (DC-STT) PDE engine (`DecoupledSpatioTemporalTransport`) + trainable PyTorch neural velocity/source predictor (`NeuralTransportFlowNet` + `train_neural_transport_model`) parameterized via streamfunction curl ($\nabla^\perp \psi_\theta$) + compressible potential gradient ($\nabla \phi_\theta$) and disocclusion source injection ($\mathbf{s}_\theta \odot \mathbf{m}_{\text{dis}}$). Spec: [`docs/DECOUPLED_CONTINUOUS_SPATIOTEMPORAL_TRANSPORT_SPEC.md`](DECOUPLED_CONTINUOUS_SPATIOTEMPORAL_TRANSPORT_SPEC.md). | `experimental` |

| [`rust/apedp-kernel/src/rime/eval.rs`](../rust/apedp-kernel/src/rime/eval.rs) | RIME scorer: ridge-fitted linear readout, charged degrees of freedom, parallel batch evaluation. | `experimental` |
| [`rust/apedp-kernel/src/rime/seeds.rs`](../rust/apedp-kernel/src/rime/seeds.rs) | Deterministic 60-form seed catalogue (levels, delays, differences, lead-lag interactions, memory, wrappers). | `experimental` |
