### APEDP-MMLM — Multi-Mathematical Language Model Architecture (experimental)

`BPEMMLMTokenizer` learns deterministic adjacent UTF-8 byte merges and preserves
case, whitespace, and unseen Unicode exactly. `max_vocab_size` includes four
special tokens and 256 byte tokens, so it must be at least 260 (for example,
308 allows up to 48 learned tokens). Encoding works before fitting using bytes;
refitting resets learned merges. The package facade also exports `ZXDiagram`,
`mmlm_zx_t_count`, and `optimize_clifford_plus_t_zx`.
`TensorTrainMemoryEngine.dmrg_two_site_sweep` fits a finite, position-dependent
MPS to equal-length sequences with target amplitudes
`sqrt(sequence frequency / sample count)`. It alternates exact two-site
least-squares projections in canonical environments and SVD truncation.
`max_bond_dim` bounds retained ranks; `cutoff` is relative to the largest
singular value. The report measures squared reconstruction error and final
normalized Schmidt spectra/entropies (natural logarithms). The fitted cores
and their compact observed-token alphabet are available as `dmrg_tensors`
and `dmrg_token_ids`. This is DMRG-style variational tensor fitting, **not**
a quantum ground-state solver or a replacement for the existing streaming
tied-core/readout predictor; that predictor is unchanged. Unequal-length
sequences and invalid token IDs fail explicitly rather than being padded or
wrapped silently.

The DQI schedule cap now comes from the existing
`dqi_official_compatibility.json` pinned-build reference measurement
(`builds.pinned.reference.qubits`), not the absent
`dqi_full_instance_reverify.json`. Its scope is the full 4,095-position
instance measured over **64 shots**, with recorded restored syndromes and
clean junk. This supplies a measured width, **not** a new full-verifier
receipt or correctness certificate. Missing evidence still raises
`UnsourcedCapError`; an explicit caller-supplied cap remains supported.

| Module | Purpose | Status |
|---|---|---|
| [`apedp_mmlm/__init__.py`](../apedp_mmlm/__init__.py) | Package facade for the three-track Multi-Mathematical Language Model research architecture (`Track A` non-neural, `Track B` ablation-governed hybrid, `Track C` attention-free replacement) and quantum discovery **Direction (owner, 2026-11-19):** MMLM is the route from language to science; Track S (`equation_language.py`) is the track with a measured result, while tracks A–C have no committed evaluation on real text. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/equation_language.py`](../apedp_mmlm/equation_language.py) | MMLM Track S: the package's engines as a language model over equations (prefix tokens in the exhaustive search's dialect, variables renamed by first appearance, constants one token), used as a prior for discovery; `search_prior` prices a candidate's structure for `exhaustive_search`. Development study, five-fold cross-validated on SRBench's 133 formulas (`scripts/study_equation_language.py`, `benchmarks/equation_language_dev_study.json`): held-out equations cost 33.7 bits under the Kneser–Ney automaton against 52.1 uniform and 40.8 unigram; of the 30 held-out laws the search can reach, ordering candidates by the model puts the law at a median position of 112 against 6268 in size order. The tensor-train engine does worse than a uniform code here (376 bits). That study was in-distribution. `SelfAdjustingEquationModel` re-weights its engines by how well each predicted every confirmed law, learns the law, and can retract one. Preregistered test [`PREREG-MMLM-S-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-MMLM-S-2026-11.md) (`scripts/run_prereg_mmlm_track_s.py`), all three expectations held: on 24 laws from other fields the model put the law at a median position of 303.5 against 6,111.5 in size order (23 earlier, 1 later); selection from data was unchanged (24 of 24 both ways, so no gain could show); on a stream of 120 invented laws the self-adjusting copy cost 53 % of a frozen copy's bits in the second half ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-MMLM-S-2026-11.md)). Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_equation_language.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/ledger_learning.py`](../apedp_mmlm/ledger_learning.py) | Track S learning from the trial ledger: `DomainLearner.sync(ledger)` feeds each domain's self-adjusting model with the laws the referee side confirmed (a `refusal_memory` confirmation round ending `unique_support`, or a `contrast_resolution` round ending `confirmed`) and unlearns on a `retracted` record. Reads only what was confirmed, where, and the law; ledger text is parsed only if it is a plain expression; idempotent. PREREG-MMLM-S2-2026-11: per-domain learning was not worse than pooled (11.59 against 11.74 bits). Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_ledger_learning.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/tensor_train_equations.py`](../apedp_mmlm/tensor_train_equations.py) | The tensor-train contraction with a readout fitted by multinomial logistic regression instead of counted. Cross-validated on SRBench's formulas it costs 40.8 bits per held-out equation where the counted readout cost 375.6 and a uniform code 52.1; that equals the unigram and is still behind the automaton (33.7). Repaired, not competitive. Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_tensor_train_equations.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/adaptive_mixture.py`](../apedp_mmlm/adaptive_mixture.py) | Text track A with self-adjusting weights: an online mixture of A0's own engines, each weight multiplied after every token by the probability that engine gave it. On real text (`scripts/study_mmlm_text.py`, `benchmarks/mmlm_text_dev_study.json`: this repository's audit prose, bits per byte) it costs 3.86 against 4.25 for A0's fixed weights, which equals the automaton alone (3.87); tracks B and C cost 4.21 and 4.20. Every track loses to gzip (2.78) and bz2 (2.26). Development study | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/law_language.py`](../apedp_mmlm/law_language.py) | MMLM-L, the scientific model: a language model over laws. Counting experts that each see one view of the partial expression tree (parent operator, grandparent, sibling already written, number of variables so far, depth, previous tokens) are mixed geometrically with weights fitted on held-out laws, per position class. Four views exist pooled and per domain; a domain expert shrinks towards the pooled expert, so a field with no laws is scored by the pooled model. Two weight sets (new domain, fitted with whole domains held out; established domain) are blended by how many laws the domain has. `canonical` orders the terms of sums and products without reference to variable names. `observe` takes one gradient step on the weights for a confirmed law and then counts it; `retract` undoes it. `search_prior` plugs into the exhaustive and guided searches. Preregistered test: [`PREREG-MMLM-L-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-MMLM-L-2026-11.md) (`scripts/run_prereg_mmlm_law.py`). Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_law_language.py` **Result (run once, all six expectations held):** on 56 ODEBench laws neither model was built on, 22.61 bits per law against 27.94 for the earlier model (cheaper on 52 of 56) and 19.27 against 24.87 while learning. As a search prior it orders candidates no better than the earlier model (median position 20 against 24.5, sign test p = 0.75), and with no laws from a field it is not better than a unigram (31.51 against 29.97) until it has learned some ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-MMLM-L-2026-11.md)). | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/law_corpora.py`](../apedp_mmlm/law_corpora.py) | Laws from outside the repository, read without executing anything: ODEBench's 63 dynamical systems (fetched once, pinned by SHA-256, cached in the ignored `data/mmlm/`), parsed with `ast` and a whitelist expression walker instead of `sympify`. Used as the fresh laws of PREREG-MMLM-L-2026-11. Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_law_language.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/law_ledger.py`](../apedp_mmlm/law_ledger.py) | Feeds one `LawLanguageModel` from the trial ledger: each referee-side confirmation is learned once under its domain, each retraction of a learned law is undone. Reads the ledger, never writes to it. Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_law_language.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/context_mixing.py`](../apedp_mmlm/context_mixing.py) | MMLM-CM, the generic model: a byte-level language model. Each byte is predicted as eight binary decisions. Experts: counts of the next bit given the previous 0 to 6 and 8 bytes and given the word so far and the previous word, over the training text; the same contexts counted over the text read so far; two match experts (the byte that followed the most recent occurrence of the last 8 or 4 bytes); and one neural expert. What a count state is worth is calibrated on held-out text, and the experts' log-odds are mixed with weights fitted by gradient descent, with separate weight sets by bit position, longest context seen, match length, kind of previous byte and recency. Any expert can be left out, so each one's contribution is measured. Vectorised: fitted on 10 MB in about a minute. Development study: `scripts/study_mmlm_cm_dev.py`. Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_context_mixing.py` **Preregistered result ([`PREREG-MMLM-CM-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-MMLM-CM-2026-11.md), run once, all four expectations held):** 1.455 bits per byte on the WikiText-2 test split against 1.513 for the best classical compressor primed with the same training text (PPMd order 16); the network alone 1.772; leaving out the network, the read-so-far counts, the match experts or the word experts each costs 0.020 to 0.025 ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-MMLM-CM-2026-11.md); `scripts/run_prereg_mmlm_cm.py`). Not compared with large neural language models, which are well ahead. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/neural_expert.py`](../apedp_mmlm/neural_expert.py) | The one neural expert of MMLM-CM: a single-layer byte LSTM trained on CPU by backpropagation through time for a wall-clock budget (`scripts/train_mmlm_neural_expert.py`), on the training text only. `bit_probabilities` returns, per byte and bit, the probability the mixer consumes. Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_context_mixing.py` The committed weights (`benchmarks/mmlm/neural_expert_wikitext2.pt`, 806,144 weights, 40 minutes on two CPU cores) are pinned by `preregistration/DIGEST-MMLM-CM.json`. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/transformer_expert.py`](../apedp_mmlm/transformer_expert.py) | A larger neural expert for MMLM-CM, for a GPU: a causal byte-level transformer trained with mixed precision for a wall-clock budget, keeping the weights that score best on held-out text. It enters the mixture as the LSTM does (`bit_probabilities`, scored in overlapping windows so every byte has at least half a window of context). Trained and measured on the development half by `scripts/train_mmlm_gpu_expert.py`, which `notebooks/mmlm_gpu_expert_colab.ipynb` runs in Colab on WikiText-103 (540 MB; same validation and test text as WikiText-2). **No GPU result yet:** checked on CPU with tiny models only. Tests: `tests/test_mmlm_transformer_expert.py` | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/text_corpus.py`](../apedp_mmlm/text_corpus.py) | WikiText-2 as bytes for MMLM-CM. **Correction 2026-11-19:** the module and the preregistration say "raw"; the files are the standard tokenised version (rare words written `<unk>`), so absolute bits per byte are not comparable with results on raw text; comparisons within the run are unaffected. The text is: fetched once from the PyTorch examples repository, pinned by SHA-256, cached in the ignored `data/mmlm/`; the validation split is cut in two halves (fit and development). The test split is read only by the preregistered run | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/engines.py`](../apedp_mmlm/engines.py) | The five mathematical engines: Engine 1 (Weighted Automata, Decomposable Probabilistic Circuits, Bayesian Dirichlet Engine), Engine 2 (trainable 1D Tensor Train streaming cores, separate two-site finite-MPS empirical-amplitude fitting & Binary Hierarchical Tree Tensor Network), Engine 3 (Streaming Dynamical Systems & EDMD Koopman Operators), Engine 4 (Concept Hypergraphs & FFT Vector Symbolic Architectures), Engine 5 (Global Sequence Energy Evaluator, Polynomial Speculative Energy Decoder, and Epistemic Adjudicator), and Byte-Pair Encoding (`BPEMMLMTokenizer`) with UTF-8 byte fallback | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/models.py`](../apedp_mmlm/models.py) | Models `NonNeuralMMLM_A0`, `HybridMMLM_B0` and `AttentionFreeMMLM_C0`, plus small comparison baselines. `Milestone1BenchmarkSuite` defaults to a deterministic synthetic subject-relation-object split (75/25/25), fits its tokenizer on training text only, and accepts an optional train/dev/test corpus carrying source, license, retrieval date, and per-split hashes. Those declarations are not independently verified, no suitable external MMLM corpus is bundled, and fixed retrieval/ListOps probes remain synthetic. H2 is measured as B0 dynamical-only streaming next-token accuracy against the frozen 0.70 threshold; H5 requires B0 or C0 to win both majority-based quality and efficiency axes against each baseline. Results and repeated-seed variability remain descriptive, not significance tests or external validation unless that data is actually supplied and audited. | `experimental` |
| [`apedp_mmlm/quantum_discovery.py`](../apedp_mmlm/quantum_discovery.py) | Quantum-reach MMLM circuit discovery: Universal ZX-Calculus & Clifford+T synthesis (`ZXSpider`, `ZXDiagram`, `optimize_clifford_plus_t_zx`, `mmlm_zx_t_count`), Local Complementation + symplectic CX graph-state synthesis (`synthesize_graph_state_mmlm`), general stabilizer tableau synthesis (`synthesize_stabilizer_target_mmlm`, `synthesize_any_target_mmlm`), commutation-aware Clifford rewriting (`mmlm_clifford_search`), DAG front-layer lookahead routing (`place_with_dag_lookahead`), fault-tolerant compilation proposers (`MMLMInvSqrtProposer`, `MMLMDQIScheduleOptimizer`, `MMLMDfthcsosOptimizer`), and sparse cubic HUBO + Koopman QAOA equation discovery (`MMLMQEDProposer`). Measured on the corpus's 75 graph states: **54 wins / 21 ties / 0 losses**, **308 two-qubit gates saved** (6,193 $\to$ 5,885) — split by family `random_graph` **25/25 wins, 238 gates**, `regular3` **17/25 wins, 29 gates**, `regular4` **12/25 wins, 41 gates**, and thinning with width on the regular families (`regular4` 0 gates at 32q, `regular3` 3 at 64q), so **77% of the total is one family** — 75/75 certified `VALID` by `adjudicate_specification`, recorded in [`benchmarks/quantum_compilation/vqc_mmlm_graph_state.json`](../benchmarks/quantum_compilation/vqc_mmlm_graph_state.json) and reproduced by [`scripts/measure_vqc_mmlm_graph_states.py`](../scripts/measure_vqc_mmlm_graph_states.py), with one measured negative result attached: a bounded plateau walk (`max_plateau_steps`, up to 8 zero-delta moves past the 2-step lookahead, LC and CX ranked together) reproduces **54 wins / 308 gates exactly**, so the thinning on `regular3`/`regular4` is a limit of the move set rather than of search depth | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/__init__.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/__init__.py) | MMLM-MATTER-001 package facade: the Phase 1 model-interoperability slice of [`docs/MMLM-MATTER-001_Implementation_Blueprint.md`](MMLM-MATTER-001_Implementation_Blueprint.md). Separate from `apedp_mmlm` (mathematical language models); nothing in the package accepts a candidate, because verification is the referee's. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/models/contract.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/models/contract.py) | `ModelContract`: the frozen provider/model/prompt-hash/schema-hash identity of one proposer arm, with drift refusals (`PromptDriftError`, `SchemaDriftError`) and `retarget()` so a model can be chosen after preregistration without disturbing the pins (blueprint §12.6, §11.1). | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/models/prompts.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/models/prompts.py) | Versioned provider-neutral P1 topology-ranking and P2 direct-design prompt templates; the system prompt and template text are hashed together so `prompt_hash` describes everything actually sent. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/models/adapters.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/models/adapters.py) | Provider-agnostic model transports: one OpenAI-compatible chat-completions adapter (OpenRouter, OpenAI, DeepSeek, NVIDIA NIM, Aihubmix, or any local vLLM/Ollama endpoint via `base_url`) and a deterministic `ReplayAdapter` over a hash-keyed JSONL cassette. A refused request, missing key or malformed body raises `ModelUnavailableError` instead of returning an empty proposal. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/models/outputs.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/models/outputs.py) | Strict Pydantic output schemas and parsers for the P1 ranking and P2 design: reject malformed JSON, unknown catalogue ids, duplicate ids, duplicate ranks, over-long selections and non-physical dimensions, each with a machine-readable reason (blueprint §10.2). | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/schemas/task_spec.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/schemas/task_spec.py) | `PublicTaskView` (everything a proposer may see), `PublicFeedback` (this run's own public attempt history) and `SealedTask` (planted topology, dimensions and hidden seed) as a type boundary, so hidden ground truth cannot be included in a prompt (blueprint §10.2, §17). | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/schemas/topology_spec.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/schemas/topology_spec.py) | `TopologyEntry`: the public catalogue metadata (id, link/joint counts, inversion, ground/input/output links, parent base chain, canonical graph hash) that P0 and P1 both receive. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/proposers/p1_llm_topology.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/proposers/p1_llm_topology.py) | P1 arm: ranks catalogue topologies under the frozen contract and returns an ordered selection plus an LLM usage record. It has no acceptance surface — no verdict field, no adjudication method (blueprint §10.2). | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/proposers/p2_llm_direct.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/proposers/p2_llm_direct.py) | P2 ablation arm: proposes a topology and dimensions with no numerical optimiser in the loop, using the same checkers and acceptance threshold as P0/P1 (blueprint §10.3). | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/evaluation/budget_accountant.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/evaluation/budget_accountant.py) | LLM usage rows (`LLMUsage`) and the append-only `BudgetLedger` for `budget_ledger.jsonl`: tokens, latency, model/prompt/schema/contract hashes and cost, with aggregation that reports completeness and keeps a provider's unreported count as `None` rather than `0` (blueprint §11.1, §11.2). | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/protocol/freeze.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/protocol/freeze.py) | The frozen Phase 1 protocol bundle (blueprint §12.6): prompt, schema and (once a model is chosen) contract hashes for P1 and P2 in one file that carries its own hash, refuses to be silently replaced, detects hand edits, and raises `ProtocolDriftError` when the checkout's prompt or schema no longer matches what was preregistered. `pin_models=False` freezes the protocol before a provider is selected. The bundle also carries the Phase 0 preregistration (below), so one file pins both the model protocol and the checker thresholds, and `verify_preregistration` refuses a bundle frozen with different ones. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/protocol/preregistration.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/protocol/preregistration.py) | The preregistered Phase 0 thresholds (blueprint §5.2, §5.7, §12.6): the Grashof safety margin and the T2 angular tolerance in one self-hashed record. `grashof()` runs the §5.2 test with the pinned margin so no call site supplies its own, `require_function_tolerance` refuses a T2 task whose declared tolerance differs from the pinned one, and the record is stored inside the frozen protocol bundle where a hand edit is detected. It also pins the measurement settings the thresholds are read under — `path_samples` (enforced by `require_path_samples`), `continuity_max_jump_deg`, `cognate_tolerance_mm` and the loop-closure tolerances — because a pass on a coarser grid is a different claim. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/fourbar.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/fourbar.py) | Checker V2 for four-bars (blueprint §5.1–§5.4): canonical coordinate convention, strict Grashof test with a caller-declared margin and full inversion classification, closed-form position analysis through the half-angle tangent that keeps both signed-square-root assembly branches, branch/circuit continuity, closed-form input and output dead centres classified separately, and coupler-path tracing. `poses()` exposes both assembly roots without choosing one; `trace` follows a circuit by joint continuity by default (`circuit=False` reproduces the old label sweep for forensics) and `pose_near`/`trace_continuous` are the primitives behind it (the branch label is not a root selector — both roots can carry it) and stop at a jump beyond a declared bound; `circuit_breaks` counts those breaks from the geometry. Referee-side: it may return a verdict, a proposer never may. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/path_metric.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/path_metric.py) | The T1 path metric (blueprint §5.6): exact discrete Fréchet distance over the ordered target and traversed coupler path, plus `check_path`, which aligns the traversed segment, refuses a reversed orientation unless the task allows it, and reports the samples and sampling metadata with the error rather than calling it a continuous certificate. A truncated trace is never scored (`partial_trace_not_scored`, `frechet_mm=None`); `refine=True` re-scores at increasing sample counts and reports whether the distance settled (`samples_tested`, `frechet_change_mm`, `refinement_stable`) with the sampling resolution `resolution_mm`; `continuity=True` judges circuit continuity from the joint angles (`Trace.continuity_tracked`) instead of the branch label; and `verify_loop_closure=True` adds a second opinion from the independent numerical solver on every pose the check accepted. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/function_metric.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/function_metric.py) | The T2 function-generation metric (blueprint §5.7): solves the mechanism at every declared input on the requested branch and reports wrapped angular error as max and RMS against the task's preregistered tolerance, rejecting unreachable inputs, branch changes and samples that do not cover the declared input range. `score_function_samples` re-scores already-solved poses, so a recorded run can be adjudicated without re-solving it, and `verify_loop_closure=True` adds the independent numerical solver's second opinion on every pose it scored, so a T2 pass does not rest on the closed form alone. The declared inputs are scored on one assembly — the first on the requested branch, each later one on the root nearest its predecessor — so two roots sharing a branch label cannot hop the score onto the other circuit. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/loop_closure.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/loop_closure.py) | The independent numerical loop-closure solver (blueprint §5.5, §5.8): damped Newton on the two loop-closure residuals, seeded from a blind grid over the joint-angle torus and sharing no algebra with the closed form, plus `cross_check`, which draws deterministic strict-Grashof crank-rockers and compares both assembly branches against the closed form at every sampled input, reporting the maximum coupler/output angle deviation and residual. `verify_poses` is the per-pose form used by the T1 path check, and every returned root is judged only on the loop-closure residual. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/cognates.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/cognates.py) | The Roberts–Chebyshev cognate construction (blueprint §5.8), derived from the loop closure rather than copied: with `w = (P−A)e^{−i theta3}` the shared focal pivot is `(w/beta)g` and the two cognates' crank/coupler/output vectors follow, so all three coupler points coincide at `P` at every instant. `check_cognates` re-solves each cognate with its *own* closed form driven by the assembly's crank angle and reports the focal-pivot drift, the coupler-point gap, the ordered Fréchet distance and the symmetric Hausdorff distance between the point *sets* (which is what "the same curve" means when each cognate is driven independently and therefore sampled differently) against the declared tolerance, plus the angular shortfall of the nearest assembly (`coupler_angle_gap_deg`, so a cognate that cannot sit at the constructed configuration reports a measured gap rather than only a reason, judged against a preregistered `cognate_angle_tolerance_deg`) and each cognate's Grashof inversion. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/topology_check.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/topology_check.py) | The topology half of a T3 answer (blueprint §2.2, §2.4, §2.5): Grübler degrees of freedom for a planar revolute chain, three distinct link roles, and the inversion the dimensions actually produce must be the inversion the catalogue entry declares. A structure this checkout cannot verify — the blueprint's 6-, 8- and 10-link universe — is refused as `topology_unsupported_structure` rather than passed on its metadata. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/boundary.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/kinematics/boundary.py) | Runtime half of the proposer/referee boundary (AGENTS.md §1.1): every public checker asks who called it and raises `BoundaryError` when the answer is `mmlm_matter_001.proposers` or `mmlm_matter_001.models`. The lint rule stops the import; this stops the accident, including a dynamic import that the build-time rule cannot see. It inspects the whole calling stack rather than only the immediate caller, and checks each frame's source path as well as its module name, so rewriting `__name__` does not launder a call. It is a boundary, not a sandbox. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/pipeline/gate.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/pipeline/gate.py) | The Phase 0 gate (blueprint §5.2, §5.6, §5.7, §12.6): runs the Grashof test with the pinned margin, scores T1 with the pinned sampling and solver tolerances (continuity-tracked roots, refinement-checked distance, loop-closure second opinion on every accepted pose), T2 against the pinned angular tolerance with the same second opinion, and T3 as topology validity plus the task's own metric, optionally adding the cognate check. A verdict is `accepted`, `refused` or `unverifiable`: an answer this checkout has no checker for is a capability gap, not a candidate failure, and the ledger counts it separately so it cannot inflate a failure rate. It returns one `GateVerdict` carrying the digests and effective settings it was judged under, refuses to run without a preregistration, refuses a sample count that is not the pinned one, refuses a bundle frozen to other thresholds, and refuses a proposer caller. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/evaluation/verdict_ledger.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/evaluation/verdict_ledger.py) | Append-only `verdicts.jsonl` of Phase 0 verdicts, kept separate from the budget ledger so cost aggregation never has to filter foreign rows: every row carries the candidate, the preregistration and protocol digests, the effective settings, the measurement records and the reasons, so a refusal can be audited after the fact instead of living only in a printed return value. `summary()` reports accepted/refused counts and per-reason tallies. | `experimental` |
| [`mmlm_matter_001/pipeline/dry_run.py`](../mmlm_matter_001/pipeline/dry_run.py) | Offline Phase 1 dry run: P1 runs against a deterministic local stub catalogue through the real prompt, parser and protocol pins, appending one `budget_ledger.jsonl` row per call. The stub names itself `stub` in every row and has zero prices, so the ledger arithmetic is exercised without a provider or a fake model identity. | `experimental` |
| [`tests/test_mmlm_matter_001_protocol_and_phase0.py`](../tests/test_mmlm_matter_001_protocol_and_phase0.py) | Pins the protocol bundle against hand edits, silent replacement and prompt/schema drift; runs the dry-run pipeline offline and checks the ledger; and covers the Phase 0 four-bar checker — Grashof strictness/margin/inversion, loop closure on both branches, unreachable inputs, circuit continuity, toggle classification, Fréchet order preservation and the T1 pass/fail boundary. | `experimental` |
| [`tests/test_mmlm_matter_001_model_interop.py`](../tests/test_mmlm_matter_001_model_interop.py) | Pins the Phase 1 model layer with no network or credentials: contract hashing and drift refusals, every P1/P2 rejection reason, sealed-task leakage guards, transport-failure refusals, replay determinism and the unpriced/unexposed accounting rules. | `experimental` |
| [`tests/test_apedp_mmlm_architecture.py`](../tests/test_apedp_mmlm_architecture.py) | Verification suite testing the mathematical engines, component ablations, and quantum discovery. Its Milestone 1 pin checks disjoint train/dev/test triples, train-only tokenizer coverage, the reported protocol metadata, and explicit `NOT_ASSESSED` outcomes for hypotheses whose metrics are absent; measured ablation comparisons are not required to pass | `experimental` |
| [`tests/test_dyn_basis_proposer_transfer.py`](../tests/test_dyn_basis_proposer_transfer.py) | Fits a polynomial basis proposer on development observations for two distinct synthetic equation families and evaluates the returned frozen scorer on disjoint out-of-range observations; this pins code-path transfer only, not empirical cross-domain science | `experimental` |

*Documentation generated for APEDP-X v2.0.0 — Autonomous Physics, Engineering & Market Discovery Platform*
### Point-in-time crypto panel and preregistration PREREG-CRYPTO-PIT-2026-11

The first run of the referee on data none of its authors has looked at. The
dataset is built by a workflow, not committed by hand, and the analysis is
frozen by digest before the dataset exists.

| Module | Role | Status |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| [`scripts/build_point_in_time_crypto_panel.py`](../scripts/build_point_in_time_crypto_panel.py) | Builds a daily panel of every Binance spot pair quoted in USDT from the public archive at data.binance.vision, including pairs that stopped trading before the end date. Lists the archive's S3 bucket, downloads each monthly kline zip, checks it against the archive's `.CHECKSUM` file, and refuses any host other than the archive and its listing. Excludes stablecoin and fiat bases (`PEGGED_BASES`) and leveraged tokens (a base ending in UP/DOWN/BULL/BEAR whose remainder is itself a listed base). Writes `panel_development.csv.gz`, `panel_holdout.csv.gz` (split at `--holdout-start`, default 2026-01-01) and `manifest.json` (the SHA-256 of every source file, the universe-selection disclosure, the pairs that stopped trading). Columns per asset: close, volume, taker-buy volume, and order-flow imbalance `(2·taker − volume)/volume`. Tests: `tests/test_point_in_time_crypto_panel.py` against a fake archive | `experimental` |
| [`scripts/run_prereg_crypto_pit.py`](../scripts/run_prereg_crypto_pit.py) | The frozen analysis. Three candidates go through `PromotionTracker`: a known effect the referee should promote (`CPLUS-VOL`, 20-day volatility → next-day absolute return), a placebo it should refuse (`CZERO-PLAC`), and the hypothesis under test (`H1-OFI`, order-flow imbalance → next-day return, sign fixed on development data). The holdout is split by date so each candidate that reaches stage 4 claims its own window in `FullLineageExperimentLedger`; candidates refused earlier never touch the holdout. Refuses to write into an existing output directory, so it runs once | `audit-only` |

The start is the competition's reference circuit unless `--start` names another; `--whole` adds the check on every input for a circuit that would lead. Writes every version with its code and the checker's words, the leading circuit's text and a `QuantumCandidateLedger` of every verifier run. `demo` and `evolve` need the competition's simulator in its pinned build | `experimental` |
| [`scripts/forecast_runs.py`](../scripts/forecast_runs.py) | The self-forecast applied to the runs on record. `recorded(group)` reads every run of a group of routes from `preregistration/RESULT-*` with its law and outcome (`tables`: the path routes on SRBench; `trajectories`: the path routes on ODEBench and the blind chaotic systems; `sparse`: the cubic sparse-regression reference). `backtest` holds one recorded run out at a time and forecasts it from the others; the record is `benchmarks/outcome_forecast_backtest.json`. `file` writes, for a list of problems and a route (a new route is forecast as an existing one, and the file says so), each problem's chances, the totals and their widened intervals: this is what is frozen with a preregistration before a run and scored after it. `score` compares every forecast filed that way (`FILED`) with its run: the totals against their intervals and against the earlier runs' averages, and run by run the Brier score and how well the chances rank the runs; the record is `benchmarks/outcome_forecast_scored.json` (two forecasts: five of six totals inside their intervals, one clear miss). Tests: `tests/test_outcome_forecast.py`, `tests/test_routes_in_force.py` | `audit-only` |
| [`scripts/build_dysts_polynomial_systems.py`](../scripts/build_dysts_polynomial_systems.py) | Writes `benchmarks/dysts_polynomial_systems.json` from the `dysts` 1.0 database of chaotic flows by a mechanical rule (autonomous, three or four variables, polynomial of degree at most four, not an ODEBench system nor one with the same terms), each system in units of its own dominant period; `--screen` lists the systems whose rows the frozen world builder does not produce within 120 s. Runs no discovery. | `audit-only` |
| [`scripts/check_recoveries_out_of_range.py`](../scripts/check_recoveries_out_of_range.py) | Audit M-23: every recovery in the recorded SRBench result sets, judged again by the benchmark's own criterion on rows from a range twice as wide in every variable (not crossing zero where the sampled range does not, and leaving out rows next to a pole of the true law). Reads the committed rows and the manifest; reruns nothing and changes no recorded result. Output `benchmarks/recoveries_out_of_range.json`: 174 of 174, 189 of 189 and 152 of 152 hold for the three routes; the two narrow passes of the language-model run fail, and so does one piecewise form of a comparison engine. `tests/test_recoveries_out_of_range.py` (7). | `audit-only` |
| [`scripts/run_blind_discovery_benchmark.py`](../scripts/run_blind_discovery_benchmark.py) | The third preregistration, [`PREREG-BLIND-L2-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-BLIND-L2-2026-11.md): how often `L2DiscoveryEngine` promotes the right law, a wrong one, or nothing. 12 textbook laws and 24 invented by a seeded generator over meaningless names and random SI dimensions (so recall cannot pass for discovery), each with an irrelevant variable, at 0/1/5/10% noise over two seeds: 288 runs, rates with 95% Clopper–Pearson intervals. Recovery means the promoted law's terms, coefficients removed, equal the truth's. Refuses to run if it or its preregistration differs from `preregistration/DIGEST-BLIND-L2.json`. Tests: `tests/test_blind_discovery_benchmark.py` (dimensional consistency of every law; a toy law end to end) | `audit-only` |
| [`scripts/fetch_srbench_ground_truth.py`](../scripts/fetch_srbench_ground_truth.py) | Builds `benchmarks/srbench/manifest.json` (SRBench's ground-truth track from PMLB: 119 Feynman equations with units and sampling ranges, 14 Strogatz ODE right-hand sides; each data file pinned by the SHA-256 of its git-LFS pointer) and downloads the data into the ignored `data/srbench/` cache, refusing any file whose hash differs. Units map kg, m, s, T, V onto `Dimension` (the volt in the `i` slot, an invertible change of basis). | `audit-only` |
| [`scripts/run_srbench_baseline.py`](../scripts/run_srbench_baseline.py) | The fourth preregistration, [`PREREG-SRBENCH-BASELINE-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-SRBENCH-BASELINE-2026-11.md): L2, RIME, RIME-AD and the extended hierarchical search on all 133 problems at 0/0.1/1/10 % noise, same rows, renamed variables, 120 s per run in its own process. Scores symbolic recovery (constants except exponents refitted to the clean truth; no term may be refitted away), accuracy (test R² ≥ 0.999) and, for L2, whether its referee promoted a right law, a wrong one, or none. Runs sharded by problem on GitHub Actions (`.github/workflows/srbench-baseline.yml`, on demand); `collect` is the only writer of the results. Tests: `tests/test_srbench_baseline.py` **Result (run once, 2026-11-19):** symbolic recovery L2 108 of 532, extended hierarchical 36, RIME 24, RIME-AD 11; the expectation was missed, 168 of L2's 268 promotions (62.7 %) were not the recovered law ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-SRBENCH-BASELINE-2026-11.md), audit M-10). | `audit-only` |
| [`scripts/run_srbench_external.py`](../scripts/run_srbench_external.py) | The fifth preregistration, [`PREREG-SRBENCH-EXTERNAL-2026-11`](../preregistration/PREREG-SRBENCH-EXTERNAL-2026-11.md): PySR, PhySO and the in-house exhaustive search on the baseline's SRBench protocol. Rows, renaming, noise, isolation and scoring are imported from `run_srbench_baseline.py` unchanged; PySR and PhySO search for 240 s (PhySO has no time stop, so its logger ends the search and the best program so far is returned), runs are killed at 330 s, noise 0 and 1 %. PySR and PhySO are not package dependencies: `.github/workflows/srbench-external.yml` installs them and runs each adapter on a toy law before the run. Frozen before any baseline result was seen. Tests: `tests/test_srbench_external.py` **Result (run once, 2026-11-19):** both expectations held; Feynman symbolic recovery of 119 at noise 0 and 1 %: PySR 73 and 68, PhySO 41 and 40, exhaustive search 31 and 31, against L2's 24 and 24 ([result](../preregistration/RESULT-SRBENCH-EXTERNAL-2026-11.md), audit M-11). | `audit-only` |
| [`scripts/check_preregistration.py`](../scripts/check_preregistration.py) | Recomputes the SHA-256 of the preregistration text, the builder and the analysis script and compares them with `preregistration/DIGEST.json`. The data workflow runs it before downloading anything | `audit-only` |
| [`.github/workflows/market-data.yml`](../.github/workflows/market-data.yml) | Runs on a push to `market-data-build` or by hand: checks the digest, builds the panel on a GitHub runner, adds the building commit and the preregistration digest to the manifest, and commits the result to the orphan branch `market-data` under `crypto_usdt_daily/` | `experimental` |

The preregistration itself is
[`preregistration/PREREG-CRYPTO-PIT-2026-11.md`](../preregistration/PREREG-CRYPTO-PIT-2026-11.md).
It states in advance what counts as the referee behaving as designed (the known
effect passes stage 4 and the placebo is refused at stage 2 or 3), states no
pass criterion for H1, and records the expectation that H1 is refused at stage 4
or 5. A change to any frozen file after the data exists is a deviation and must
be reported as one.

**Result (2026-11-17), run once:**
[`preregistration/RESULT-CRYPTO-PIT-2026-11.md`](../preregistration/RESULT-CRYPTO-PIT-2026-11.md).
The known effect passed stage 4 (January 2026 holdout IC 0.426, p = 2.2e-15) and
the placebo was refused at stage 2, so the referee behaved as designed by the
preregistered criterion. The order-flow hypothesis was refused at stage 2 on
development data (|IC| 0.0013 against a 0.02 bar) and never reached the
holdout. Built under one recorded deviation
([`DEVIATION-01`](../preregistration/DEVIATION-01-2026-11-17-builder-url-encoding.md):
the builder could not fetch a pair with a non-ASCII symbol; fixed before any
data existed).

**Calibration (2026-11-17), run once:**
[`preregistration/RESULT-CRYPTO-CAL-2026-11.md`](../preregistration/RESULT-CRYPTO-CAL-2026-11.md).
On the real development panel, 0 of 500 placebos passed stage 2 (95% upper
bound 0.74%); injected effects passed stage 2 at 1% (ρ = 0.01), 55% (0.02),
97% (0.03) and 100% (0.05); one-day reversal passed stage 2 and was refused at
stage 3 as a literature factor. All three preregistered expectations held.

## What this repository cannot certify about itself

Every referee named in this index — the stabilizer and dense tiers, Stim, MQT QCEC, the
canonical ledger, the DSR machinery, the burn-log seal — was chosen, installed, wired and
interpreted inside this repository. That gives an unusually strong *internal* adversary (a
wrong circuit, a snooped holdout, an uncharged fit each fail loudly) and no *external* one:
there is no human peer review of the records and no independent replication.

Three limits follow, stated so they are not mistaken for gaps a code change would close:

- **Tamper-evidence is local.** [`holdout_seal.py`](../apedp_dyn/epistemic/holdout_seal.py)
  detects truncation, an edited burn and a rewind, and its digest is pinned by a test, so a
  rewind has to change a test to pass. Someone who rewrites both the log and the pin defeats
  it; no key and no external witness is involved. An append-only remote store
  (`FullLineageExperimentLedger(storage_backend=...)`) is the stronger form and is available,
  but is not the default.
- **Referees share a bridge.** The stabilizer and dense tiers share `gates.matrix_for` and
  the parser; Stim and QCEC are reached through this package's own exporters. A shared
  translation bug would be invisible to every tier at once, which is why the agreement
  corpora are reported as conformance samples.
- **The registry is prose as well as data.** The checks enforce that every module is
  indexed, that status tags match docstring `Status:` lines, and that links resolve — not
  that every sentence is true. Measurements are pinned by tests; the narrative around them
  is not.

### 5.25 `apedp_dyn.epistemic.discovery_envelope` — Admission Without Verification

**Module:** `apedp_dyn/epistemic/discovery_envelope.py`

**Status:** `experimental`

**Description:** Wraps a candidate that passed every gate in the only two states the
repository allows it to have: `refused`, or `awaiting_external_verification`. There is no
`verified` state. The envelope carries the gates that actually ran, the measured evidence,
a required non-empty list of claims the gates do **not** establish, and an
`ExternalVerificationRequest` naming the measurement, its acceptance condition, and why it
cannot be run in this environment.

Admission is not confirmation: `admit_for_external_verification` refuses a candidate with
no gate, no stated limit, or no external request, because each of those omissions turns a
plausible candidate into a claim nobody can check. A failing gate yields `refused` and
names the gate. This is the shape an honest discovery claim takes here — candidate,
numbers, limits, and the experiment a human must run.

**Tests:** `tests/test_discovery_envelope.py` (in the AGENTS.md standard suite) pins the
four refusals, the absence of a verified state, and one worked case built from a real
`MMLMTrackA` result rather than a fixture.

**References:** `docs/SCAFFOLD_INVENTORY.md` ("Discovery envelopes: admitted, never
verified"); `apedp_dyn/epistemic/experiment_ledger.py` for the lifecycle states this sits
above.
